{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"`minimal prompt → single careful diff pass → ≤15 findings`\n\nYou are reviewing a pull request for real bugs. Run `git diff @{upstream}...HEAD` (or `git diff main...HEAD` / `git diff HEAD~1`\nif there's no upstream) to get the unified diff under review. If there are\nuncommitted changes, or the range diff is empty, also run `git diff HEAD` and\ninclude the working-tree changes in scope — the review often runs before the\ncommit. If a PR number, branch name, or file path was passed as an argument,\nreview that target instead. Treat this diff as the review scope.\n\nReview the diff as a careful senior engineer would: read every hunk, open the surrounding files for context as needed (Read, Grep, git log/blame/show), and hunt for correctness issues — wrong or inverted conditions, off-by-one, null/undefined dereference, missing `await`, dropped error handling, removed guards or validations, broken callers of changed functions, races. Prefer real failure modes over style; every finding needs a concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves.\n\nWhen you are done, submit at most 15 findings via the ReportFindings tool, filling its fields as defined — for each: the file path and start line, a severity, and a comment that states the issue and the concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves. Quality over quantity: include everything you genuinely believe is a real issue, and nothing you don't.\n\nAfter the tool call, also restate the findings in your final reply — one line each, `file:line — summary` — so they stay visible in sessions that do not render tool output.\n"},"isMeta":true,"uuid":"3124b4c3-11df-4e9e-a14c-58a7330a5abe","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:22:26.235Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"3d47babd-d719-469b-9491-c02f62caff5a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- adversarial-review: Write and bound an adversarial review round — the brief, the stop condition, and the escape hatch. Use when commissioning a cold or adversarial review of a diff, branch or PR; when writing the brief for a review agent (codex-reviewer, grok-reviewer, a Claude critic); when a review has returned DO NOT SHIP and you are about to run another round; or when deciding whether a review loop should end. A review's productivity is a property of its BRIEF, not of its reviewer — an unbounded \"what is broken?\" brief cannot terminate, and permission to stop is not a stop condition.\n- context7-cli: Use when the user mentions \"ctx7\" or \"context7\", needs current docs for any library, wants to install/search/generate skills, or needs to set up Context7 for their AI coding agent. Fetches library documentation, manages AI coding skills, and configures Context7 MCP.\n- devcontainer-workflow: Bring the devcontainer up/down/build, run tier 1-3 smoke checks, and respect the thin host-user overlay invariant. Use when working with .devcontainer/, mise tasks, or the @devcontainers/cli.\n- find-docs: Use when the user asks about a specific library, framework, SDK, CLI tool, or cloud service -- even for well-known ones like React, Next.js, Prisma, Express, Tailwind, Django, or Spring Boot. Retrieves up-to-date documentation, API references, and code examples. Training data may not reflect recent API changes or version updates.\nAlways use for: API syntax questions, configuration options, version migration issues, \"how do I\" questions mentioning a library name, debugging that involves library-specific behavior, setup instructions, and CLI tool usage.\nUse even when you think you know the answer -- do not rely on training data for API details, signatures, or configuration options as they are frequently outdated. Always verify against current docs. Prefer this over web search for library documentation and API details.\n- graphify: Use for any question about a codebase, its architecture, file relationships, or project content — especially when graphify-out/ exists, where the question should be treated as a graphify query first. Turns any input (code, docs, papers, images, videos) into a persistent knowledge graph with god nodes, community detection, and query/path/explain tools.\n- lint-delta: Partition linter violations into yours and the upgrade's by running the previous pinned version against the same tree, via `mise run lint-delta`. Reach for it the moment a ruff, ty or other linter bump turns a green gate red — before fixing anything and before reaching for a suppression — and whenever a Renovate tool-bump PR arrives with a wall of new diagnostics. Newly-enabled rules look exactly like regressions in your own change — one measured bump took a tree from 2 violations to 138 without a line of code changing.\n- lock-image: Regenerate the devcontainer IMAGE lockfiles (`.devcontainer/mise-system.lock` + `mise-runtime.lock`) via `mise run lock-image`. Use whenever a change to `.devcontainer/mise-system.toml`, `.devcontainer/mise-runtime.toml` or `.config/mise/conf.d/shared.toml` needs its locks refreshed, when `mise run lint`'s `mise_lock_integrity` step or CI reports the image locks stale or short, or when you are about to transcribe the recipe out of `.github/actions/lock-refresh/action.yml` by hand. Reach for it BEFORE hand-rolling `mise lock` against a staged config — regenerating these two files on macOS truncates them silently, and the tool count does not move, so the damage reads as success.\n- mcp2cli: Invoke MCP servers as one-shot CLI commands (no native registration, no schema injection into Claude's context). Use whenever a research or tool-use task wants an MCP server's capabilities without paying the per-conversation context tax of `claude mcp add`.\n- memory-index-curation: Use when curating the auto-memory index (MEMORY.md) — trimming a bloated hook, deleting a stale memory, or reacting to a size warning. Shortening a hook silently destroys any fact that lives only there, so the order is verify → migrate/correct → THEN shorten. Run `mise run memory-index` first.\n- pr-workflow: Ship, automerge and land PRs via `mise run ship` / `mise run automerge -- <PR#>` / `mise run land -- <PR#>` — the full gated loop from committed work to merged-and-locally-validated main. Use whenever committing work that should become a PR, when asked to merge a green PR, when a bot-opened PR (Renovate / the refresh bot / a dependency bump) needs merging, or when validating that the ship/land wiring is intact. Never hand-roll commit→push→PR→merge sequences.\n- reap: Clear a pile of wedged processes safely via `mise run reap` — a dry-run-by-default reaper with ancestor-chain protection, an age floor, and TERM-then-KILL escalation. Reach for it whenever a host has accumulated stuck or duplicated processes (hung `git`, `fnox`, shims, orphaned workers), when load, PID or memory pressure needs a bulk cleanup, and above all before hand-rolling a `pkill`, a `ps | grep | awk | xargs kill`, or a throwaway kill script — that improvisation nearly killed a session's own shell and is why this exists. Also use it to measure a pile before deciding anything, since a dry run signals nothing and prints the full plan.\n- session-review: Find what a session did BY HAND that should have become a skill, mise task or python module, via `mise run session-review`. Use at the end of a working session, during `/clear-prep` or handoff writing, when a task felt like a slog and you want to know whether it was one, or when deciding what automation to build next. Two disjoint lanes — a transcript mine for recurring command shapes and a narrative pass over the notepad and handoff for reasoning sinks that leave no repeated command. Run it before writing the handoff, while the session's own notes are still in front of you.\n- token-check: Prove a candidate contract token binds exactly one site BEFORE writing the contract, via `mise run token-check -- <file> \"<token>\"...`. Use whenever adding or editing a `[[suite]]` in `python/verification/suites.toml`, choosing `per_path_tokens` for a new gate, or reacting to a `contract_token_uniqueness` / `token-audit` finding. Run it while picking the tokens, not after — a token matching more than once can be satisfied by a stand-in, so the contract silently asserts less than it claims, and that is how a deleted registration stayed green.\n- tool-currency-check: Use when auditing whether the repo's pinned tools are current AND whether any hand-rolled custom code is now superseded by a native tool feature. Produces a retire/bump report from mise outdated + pkl/pin-vs-latest diff + a cache-first release-note scan.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-0132c855: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-055a35fb: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-23dbefc0: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-3320bb98: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-3c14b395: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-629c12ca: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-867c3b28: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-b465f22c: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-eb160c23: Description delivered above.\n- memory-index-curation-skill-ee2ce72c: Description delivered above.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- hookify:configure: Enable or disable hookify rules interactively\n- hookify:help: Get help with the hookify plugin\n- hookify:hookify: Create hooks to prevent unwanted behaviors from conversation analysis or explicit instructions\n- hookify:list: List all configured hookify rules\n- context7:docs: Look up documentation for any library\n- skill-creator:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- hookify:writing-rules: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"create a hookify rule\", \"write a hook rule\", \"configure hookify\", \"add a hookify rule\", or needs guidance on hookify rule syntax and patterns.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- context7:context7-mcp: This skill should be used when the user asks about libraries, frameworks, API references, or needs code examples. Activates for setup questions, code generation involving libraries, or mentions of specific frameworks like React, Vue, Next.js, Prisma, Supabase, etc.\n- last30days:last30days: Research what people actually say about any topic in the last 30 days. Pulls posts and engagement from Reddit, X, YouTube, TikTok, Hacker News, Polymarket, GitHub, and the web. Includes a doctor health check to diagnose broken or missing sources.\n- exa:agent: Use Exa Agent for multi-step web research, list-building, enrichment, structured output, run continuation, and coverage validation. Exa Agent can access additional data providers: fiber, financial_datasets, similarweb, baselayer, affiliate, particle, and jinko.\n- exa:search: Deep research powered by Exa. Use for lead generation, literature reviews, deep dives, competitive analysis, or any query where one search falls short, including phrases like 'research this', 'find everything about', 'find me all', or 'deep dive on'.\n- builder-skills:agent-watchdog: Use when asked to watch, babysit, audit, review, compare, or fix another agent's work from a Codex session ID, Claude Code session/transcript, chat/thread link, PR, branch, log, or pasted run summary. Monitor until the other agent is done or blocked, reconstruct what the user asked, inspect what the agent actually changed and verified, report gaps, and optionally make scoped fixes when the user authorizes repair.\n- builder-skills:efficient-fable: Use when running Claude Fable on codebase-heavy or token-heavy work and the user wants Fable to orchestrate research, coding, and testing while cheaper subagents do bounded heavy lifting.\n- builder-skills:efficient-frontier: Apply the same orchestration as `/efficient-fable` to any high-cost frontier model: delegate research, coding, and testing to cheaper subagents while keeping planning, synthesis, and final review with the expensive model.\n- builder-skills:plan-arbiter: Use when asked to compare, cross-review, merge, judge, choose, or arbitrate competing plans from multiple agents such as Codex and Claude Code; when given two or more proposed plans, session IDs, transcripts, plan documents, PR descriptions, or pasted strategies; or when the user wants one recommended execution plan after agents review each other's proposals.\n- builder-skills:plow-ahead: Use when the user explicitly wants autonomous progress without routine clarification stops: \"plow ahead\", \"do not stop\", \"use your best judgment\", \"keep going until done\", \"finish while I am away\", \"do not ask questions unless truly blocked\", or similar. Convert ordinary ambiguity into stated assumptions, proceed through implementation and validation, stop only for true blockers, and end with a clear recap of decisions, changes, verification, and residual risk.\n- builder-skills:quick-recap: Use when adding or following the red/yellow/green final status block convention for agent responses, especially by installing managed AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md instructions.\n- builder-skills:read-the-damn-docs: Use when implementing, integrating, upgrading, debugging, or answering anything involving third-party APIs, libraries, frameworks, CLIs, cloud services, model/provider SDKs, fast-moving product behavior, user requests for latest/current/official behavior, unfamiliar repo docs/specs, errors that may indicate API drift, or high-stakes auth, security, billing, data, migration, deployment, compliance, or privacy behavior. Forces Codex to web-search for current official docs and read primary docs before assuming from memory.\n- builder-skills:rewind: Retrieve recent local Clips Rewind context when the user says \"Look at Rewind,\" asks what just happened, or refers to something they recently said or saw.\n- builder-skills:stay-within-limits: Use when long-running or parallel agent work must respect 5-hour and weekly usage limits by checking usage between waves, pausing near the cap, and resuming only when the window is clear.\n- builder-skills:visual-edit: Open a running local app in Design overview mode as URL-backed iframe screens for visual editing, flow review, duplication, and route-state exploration. Use when the user asks to inspect, compare, or edit a real local app visually in Design.\n- builder-skills:visual-plan: Turn ordinary text plans into rich interactive visual plans with diagrams, file maps, annotated code, open questions, and UI/prototype review when useful.\n- builder-skills:visual-recap: Turn a PR, branch, commit, or git diff into an interactive visual recap with diagrams, file maps, API/schema summaries, annotated diffs, and focused review notes.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. {"parentUuid":"b3fd6346-e1cc-4998-9b82-c3859dcffdd1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016tCte2GnHfd9PJDSrUaDJD","type":"tool_result","content":"commit a126a64d6af51eb03f28324a067d4465879a88d2\nAuthor: Raymond Manaloto <ray.manaloto@gmail.com>\nDate:   Mon Aug 10 01:22:14 2026 -0500\n\n    fix(image): build each architecture with its own tools and packages (#698)\n    \n    #676 published an arm64 image; its CONTENTS were still amd64. Three\n    independent causes, each of which survived the build because every\n    build-time self-check counted or stat'd rather than executed.\n    \n    1. `mise-system.toml` pinned `arch = \"x86_64\"`, so any image resolved\n       x86_64 tools regardless of its build target. Deleted: each matrix leg\n       builds inside a container of its own architecture, so mise's native\n       detection already IS the target and a literal can only disagree with\n       it. `lockfile_platforms` now covers every published architecture, and\n       both image locks were regenerated in-container — system 0 -> 132\n       linux-arm64 entries, runtime 0 -> 35, tool counts unchanged (49/22)\n       and macos-x64 coverage preserved.\n    \n    2. Two hand-downloaded single-architecture `.deb`s. Probed upstream\n       before implementing, per the ticket's first AC: graphviz publishes no\n       Linux arm64 deb (44 files in the 15.1.0 release; its only arm64\n       artefact is a macOS .pkg, which is also the control arm proving the\n       negative is real), and gcc-latest publishes none BY POLICY — the\n       maintainer states \"only for x86_64\" on the index page. So graphviz\n       falls back to apt on other architectures, and arm64 ships without\n       gcc-latest. Ray's call, 2026-08-10, recorded as D31 in the spec: the\n       image is deliberately NOT architecture-symmetric, arm64's modern-GCC\n       slot goes to conda-forge gcc (linux-aarch64: 204 files at 16.1.0,\n       identical coverage to linux-64), and \"exactly 3 gcc compilers\"\n       becomes an arch-dependent invariant.\n    \n    3. Nothing executed a mise-installed binary. A wrong-architecture tool\n       downloads, installs, lists and stats exactly like a correct one; the\n       only thing it cannot do is run. `ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` runs six of\n       them, spanning backends because the backend is what resolves the\n       platform. It asserts the capability rather than sniffing a symptom\n       (probes-need-a-control-arm rule 9) — no uname compare, no ELF read,\n       no version string, each of which would bind something upstream owns.\n    \n    Gates: `find_lock_platform_drift` + `find_pinned_image_arch` wired into\n    the existing platform-literals CLI (both FAILED on the tree before the\n    fix); `platforms_to_lock` unions the committed lock with what the\n    publish matrix owes, closing #698's ordering trap where the regen meant\n    to add an architecture would skip it for having no entries yet; contract\n    `build.image-architecture-integrity` binds all nine wiring sites.\n    \n    `build_smoke_script` grew past ruff's PLR0913 threshold, which was a\n    fair signal — `emulated` and `gcc_latest` are both facts about one\n    architecture arriving from one platform triple, so they are now an\n    `ImageArch` value with a `for_platform` constructor.\n    \n    Verified: lint rc=0, pytest 1998 passed, verify 129 passed / 0 failed,\n    pin-actions rc=0. arm64 apt pins probed with both arms — real pins PASS,\n    an injected bogus version FAILs. Mutation-tested the TARGETARCH gate:\n    deleting one `ARG TARGETARCH` fails exactly one test.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01MCFymQ3miUyFFWvQ6fouQm\n\n .devcontainer/AGENTS.md                      |    2 +-\n .devcontainer/Dockerfile                     |  116 +-\n .devcontainer/mise-runtime.lock              |  750 +++++\n .devcontainer/mise-system.lock               | 3820 ++++++++++++++++++++++++--\n .devcontainer/mise-system.toml               |   29 +-\n docs/specs/devcontainer-gcc162-dual-arch.md  |   47 +\n python/src/dotfiles_setup/image.py           |   79 +-\n python/src/dotfiles_setup/image_lock.py      |   28 +-\n python/src/dotfiles_setup/platform_target.py |  143 +-\n python/verification/suites.toml              |   48 +-\n tests/test_image_arch.py                     |  262 ++\n tests/test_image_lock.py                     |   42 +-\n tests/test_image_smoke.py                    |   18 +-\n tests/test_platform_target.py                |  111 +\n 14 files changed, 5161 insertions(+), 334 deletions(-)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"dd7d435a-4e6a-4055-9531-937a0870a90e","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:22:53.805Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b3fd6346-e1cc-4998-9b82-c3859dcffdd1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

Mutation-tested the TARGETARCH gate:\n    deleting one `ARG TARGETARCH` fails exactly one test.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01MCFymQ3miUyFFWvQ6fouQm\n\ndiff --git a/.devcontainer/AGENTS.md b/.devcontainer/AGENTS.md\nindex c9dfb6c..c16f1fd 100644\n--- a/.devcontainer/AGENTS.md\n+++ b/.devcontainer/AGENTS.md\n@@ -21,7 +21,7 @@ Defines the devcontainer image and runtime lifecycle. Two layers:\n | `devcontainer.json` | Devcontainer spec (containers.dev) — lifecycle hooks, features, volumes, dynamic naming |\n | `mise-system.toml` | BASE tool tier (#160 T9) → `/usr/local/share/mise/config.toml`. `[bootstrap.packages]` declares the apt set installed by `mise bootstrap packages apply` (#160 T4); the 20 host↔image shared tools come from the repo `.config/mise/conf.d/shared.toml` COPYd to `conf.d/` and merged (#160 T5) |\n | `mise-runtime.toml` | RUNTIME tool tier (#160 T9/T10) → `config.runtime.toml`, installed in the `devcontainer-runtime` stage under `MISE_ENV=runtime` (baked ENV). The interactive OVERLAY tier lives in `home/dot_config/mise/config.toml.tmpl`, eager-installed per-user by `on-create.sh` |\n-| `mise-system.lock` + `mise-runtime.lock` + `P2996-CACHE.md` | Native mise lockfiles (rattler conda sha256 + version pins, linux-x64) per tier; COPYd to `mise.lock` / `mise.runtime.lock` beside the configs, consumed by `mise install --system --locked`; base lock digest feeds the base content-hash, runtime pair feeds dev-hash. Regenerate via the CI `lock-refresh` job |\n+| `mise-system.lock` + `mise-runtime.lock` + `P2996-CACHE.md` | Native mise lockfiles (rattler conda sha256 + version pins) per tier, per tool **per published arch** (`linux-x64` + `linux-arm64` since #698, via `lockfile_platforms`). COPYd to `mise.lock` / `mise.runtime.lock`, consumed by `mise install --system --locked`; base digest feeds base-hash, runtime pair feeds dev-hash. Regenerate via `mise run lock-image`, never hand-rolled `mise lock` (#650) |\n \n ## Devcontainer Lifecycle\n \ndiff --git a/.devcontainer/Dockerfile b/.devcontainer/Dockerfile\nindex 61753c8..c778ccc 100644\n--- a/.devcontainer/Dockerfile\n+++ b/.devcontainer/Dockerfile\n@@ -230,6 +230,12 @@ RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/var/cache/apt,sharing=locked \\\n ARG GRAPHVIZ_VERSION=15.1.0\n ARG GRAPHVIZ_DEB=ubuntu_26.04_graphviz-15.1.0-cmake.deb\n ARG GRAPHVIZ_DEB_SHA256=f3294aeea3618044022874823344a1154ee7272857e82f248610fcaa7d2dc4c5\n+# BuildKit's automatic target-architecture arg (#698). DECLARED, never defaulted:\n+# an automatic ARG a stage does not declare expands to the EMPTY STRING, so an\n+# undeclared TARGETARCH would send every architecture — amd64 included — down\n+# the else-branch below and silently drop graphviz from the image it works on.\n+# Gated by `test_every_stage_that_reads_targetarch_also_declares_it`.\n+ARG TARGETARCH\n # Re-assert the stage shell: hadolint 2.15.x forgets a stage's SHELL after any\n # mid-stage ARG (minimal repro: FROM + SHELL bash + ARG X=y + RUN with a bashism\n # flags SC3011; drop the ARG and it is clean) and then wrongly lints later RUNs\n@@ -243,22 +249,36 @@ SHELL [\"/bin/bash\", \"-o\", \"pipefail\", \"-c\"]\n # devcontainer-base lacks libltdl7 once apt:graphviz is gone → dot would fail to\n # start); libgd3+libcairo2 back the gd plugin (png/gif); libgts backs the neato/\n # fdp/circo/twopi layout engines. Verified on a bare ubuntu:26.04 amd64 base.\n+#\n+# amd64 ONLY (#698). Upstream publishes four Linux debs — ubuntu_22.04 through\n+# ubuntu_26.04 — and every one is amd64; the only arm64 artefact in the 15.1.0\n+# release is a macOS .pkg (probed 2026-08-10, GitLab package 62359347, 44 files).\n+# So other architectures take apt's graphviz, which is the lean floor this deb\n+# was chosen to beat by ~21 MB in the first place. The version assertion follows\n+# the branch: amd64 asserts the pinned major, apt asserts what dpkg says it just\n+# installed — two independent sources either way, never a restated literal.\n RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/var/cache/apt,sharing=locked \\\n     --mount=type=cache,target=/var/lib/apt/lists,sharing=locked \\\n     export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive && \\\n-    curl -fLSs -o \"/tmp/${GRAPHVIZ_DEB}\" \"https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/4207231/packages/generic/graphviz-releases/${GRAPHVIZ_VERSION}/${GRAPHVIZ_DEB}\" && \\\n-    echo \"${GRAPHVIZ_DEB_SHA256}  /tmp/${GRAPHVIZ_DEB}\" | sha256sum -c - && \\\n     apt-get update && \\\n-    apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \"/tmp/${GRAPHVIZ_DEB}\" libltdl7 libexpat1 zlib1g libgd3 libgts-0.7-5t64 libcairo2 && \\\n-    rm -f \"/tmp/${GRAPHVIZ_DEB}\" && \\\n-    rm -f /usr/lib/graphviz/libgvplugin_{ascii,devil,gdk,gs,kitty,lasi,pango,poppler,rsvg,vt,webp,xlib}.so.8 && \\\n-    rm -f /usr/lib/graphviz/config8 && \\\n+    if [ \"${TARGETARCH}\" = \"amd64\" ]; \\\n+    else \\\n+      apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends graphviz && \\\n+      expected_major=\"$(dpkg-query -W -f='${Version}' graphviz | sed 's/^[0-9]*://; s/\\..*//')\"; \\\n+    fi && \\\n     dot -c && \\\n     printf 'digraph{a->b->c;a->c}' | dot -Tsvg -o /dev/null && \\\n     printf 'digraph{a->b}' | dot -Tpng -o /dev/null && \\\n     printf 'graph{a--b--c--a}' | neato -Tsvg -o /dev/null && \\\n     dot_version=\"$(dot -V 2>&1)\" && \\\n-    case \"$dot_version\" in *\"version 15\"*) ;; exit 1; }\n \n+# Architecture EXECUTION check (#698). Every self-check above counts or stats:\n+# `mise ls --installed | wc -l`, `test -d`, a non-empty shims dir. None of them\n+# can see the defect this exists for — a tool resolved for the wrong platform\n+# downloads, installs, lists and stats exactly like a correct one. The only\n+# thing it cannot do is RUN, so this runs it.\n+#\n+# Assert the capability, never a symptom ([[probes-need-a-control-arm]] rule 9):\n+# no `uname -m` comparison, no ELF-header sniffing, no version string — each of\n+# those binds something we do not own and goes quietly dead when it changes.\n+# A binary that executes on this CPU is the whole claim.\n+#\n+# The names are cross-checked against `.config/mise/conf.d/shared.toml` by\n+# `test_every_execution_probe_is_a_tool_the_image_installs`, so a tool rename\n+# fails a unit test rather than a 30-minute base build. They deliberately span\n+# backends (aqua/ubi/github/cargo) — the backend is what resolves the platform,\n+# so one probe per download path is what makes this more than a spot check.\n+ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES=\"jq shfmt hk pkl uv chezmoi\"\n+RUN checked=0; \\\n+    for probe in ${ARCH_EXEC_PROBES}; do \\\n+      bin=\"/usr/local/share/mise/shims/${probe}\"; \\\n+      test -x \"$bin\" || { echo \"ERROR: expected mise shim ${probe} is missing\"; exit 1; }; \\\n+      \"$bin\" --version >/dev/null || { echo \"ERROR: ${probe} INSTALLED but did not execute on $(uname -m) — its tool was resolved for another architecture (#698)\"; exit 1; }; \\\n+      checked=$((checked + 1)); \\\n+    done; \\\n+    test \"$checked\" -ge 3 || { echo \"ERROR: architecture execution check ran only ${checked} probe(s) — ARCH_EXEC_PROBES is empty or was not expanded\"; exit 1; }; \\\n+    echo \"OK: ${checked} mise-installed tools execute natively on $(uname -m)\"\n+\n # Final global permissions check\n RUN chmod -R 777 /usr/local/share/mise /usr/local/share/cargo /usr/local/share/rustup\n \n@@ -493,6 +540,19 @@ ARG GCC_LATEST_DEB=gcc-latest_17.0.0-20260719git6d5d980f76c3.deb\n # stays byte-exact without the old human-recompute friction. {"parentUuid":"3921ba03-327a-40cb-9ed9-55e6cd731cc4","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C6dyEvgAmEDMwKVRiBxY3V","type":"tool_result","content":"commit a126a64d6af51eb03f28324a067d4465879a88d2\nAuthor: Raymond Manaloto <ray.manaloto@gmail.com>\nDate:   Mon Aug 10 01:22:14 2026 -0500\n\n    fix(image): build each architecture with its own tools and packages (#698)\n    \n    #676 published an arm64 image; its CONTENTS were still amd64. Three\n    independent causes, each of which survived the build because every\n    build-time self-check counted or stat'd rather than executed.\n    \n    1. `mise-system.toml` pinned `arch = \"x86_64\"`, so any image resolved\n       x86_64 tools regardless of its build target. Deleted: each matrix leg\n       builds inside a container of its own architecture, so mise's native\n       detection already IS the target and a literal can only disagree with\n       it. `lockfile_platforms` now covers every published architecture, and\n       both image locks were regenerated in-container — system 0 -> 132\n       linux-arm64 entries, runtime 0 -> 35, tool counts unchanged (49/22)\n       and macos-x64 coverage preserved.\n    \n    2. Two hand-downloaded single-architecture `.deb`s. Probed upstream\n       before implementing, per the ticket's first AC: graphviz publishes no\n       Linux arm64 deb (44 files in the 15.1.0 release; its only arm64\n       artefact is a macOS .pkg, which is also the control arm proving the\n       negative is real), and gcc-latest publishes none BY POLICY — the\n       maintainer states \"only for x86_64\" on the index page. So graphviz\n       falls back to apt on other architectures, and arm64 ships without\n       gcc-latest. Ray's call, 2026-08-10, recorded as D31 in the spec: the\n       image is deliberately NOT architecture-symmetric, arm64's modern-GCC\n       slot goes to conda-forge gcc (linux-aarch64: 204 files at 16.1.0,\n       identical coverage to linux-64), and \"exactly 3 gcc compilers\"\n       becomes an arch-dependent invariant.\n    \n    3. Nothing executed a mise-installed binary. A wrong-architecture tool\n       downloads, installs, lists and stats exactly like a correct one; the\n       only thing it cannot do is run. `ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` runs six of\n       them, spanning backends because the backend is what resolves the\n       platform. It asserts the capability rather than sniffing a symptom\n       (probes-need-a-control-arm rule 9) — no uname compare, no ELF read,\n       no version string, each of which would bind something upstream owns.\n    \n    Gates: `find_lock_platform_drift` + `find_pinned_image_arch` wired into\n    the existing platform-literals CLI (both FAILED on the tree before the\n    fix); `platforms_to_lock` unions the committed lock with what the\n    publish matrix owes, closing #698's ordering trap where the regen meant\n    to add an architecture would skip it for having no entries yet; contract\n    `build.image-architecture-integrity` binds all nine wiring sites.\n    \n    `build_smoke_script` grew past ruff's PLR0913 threshold, which was a\n    fair signal — `emulated` and `gcc_latest` are both facts about one\n    architecture arriving from one platform triple, so they are now an\n    `ImageArch` value with a `for_platform` constructor.\n    \n    Verified: lint rc=0, pytest 1998 passed, verify 129 passed / 0 failed,\n    pin-actions rc=0. arm64 apt pins probed with both arms — real pins PASS,\n    an injected bogus version FAILs. Mutation-tested the TARGETARCH gate:\n    deleting one `ARG TARGETARCH` fails exactly one test.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01MCFymQ3miUyFFWvQ6fouQm\n\ndiff --git a/python/src/dotfiles_setup/image.py b/python/src/dotfiles_setup/image.py\nindex 8d7a359..5588580 100644\n--- a/python/src/dotfiles_setup/image.py\n+++ b/python/src/dotfiles_setup/image.py\n@@ -24,6 +24,7 @@ from dotfiles_setup.platform_target import (\n     is_emulated,\n     platform_arch,\n     resolve_platform,\n+    ships_gcc_latest,\n )\n \n if TYPE_CHECKING:\n@@ -487,8 +488,14 @@ clang++ -fuse-ld=lld /tmp/sanitizer.cpp -o /tmp/lld-linked \\\n /tmp/lld-linked >/dev/null || { echo \"FAIL: lld-linked binary did not run\"; exit 1; }\n echo \"OK: lld links (-fuse-ld=lld) + binary runs\"\n echo \"=== reflection compiler checks ===\"\n-test -x /opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++ \\\n-  || { echo \"FAIL: gcc-latest binary missing\"; exit 1; }\n+if [ -n \"$GCC_LATEST_PRESENT\" ]; then\n+  test -x /opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++ \\\n+    || { echo \"FAIL: gcc-latest binary missing\"; exit 1; }\n+else\n+  echo \"SKIP: gcc-latest not shipped on this architecture (upstream is x86_64-only)\"\n+  test ! -e /opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++ \\\n+    || { echo \"FAIL: gcc-latest present where it is declared unshipped\"; exit 1; }\n+fi\n test -x /opt/clang-p2996/bin/clang++ \\\n   || { echo \"FAIL: clang-p2996 missing\"; exit 1; }\n echo \"=== clang-p2996 ref pin check ===\"\n@@ -544,9 +551,11 @@ if [ -z \"$P2996_LIBCXX_SO\" ]; then\n   echo \"FAIL: clang-p2996 libc++.so.1 not found in image\"; exit 1\n fi\n P2996_LIBCXX_DIR=$(dirname \"$P2996_LIBCXX_SO\")\n-/opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++ -std=c++26 -freflection /tmp/refl-func.cpp -o /tmp/refl-gcc \\\n-  || { echo \"FAIL: gcc-latest reflection link failed\"; exit 1; }\n-/tmp/refl-gcc || { echo \"FAIL: gcc-latest reflection binary did not run\"; exit 1; }\n+if [ -n \"$GCC_LATEST_PRESENT\" ]; then\n+  /opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++ -std=c++26 -freflection /tmp/refl-func.cpp -o /tmp/refl-gcc \\\n+    || { echo \"FAIL: gcc-latest reflection link failed\"; exit 1; }\n+  /tmp/refl-gcc || { echo \"FAIL: gcc-latest reflection binary did not run\"; exit 1; }\n+fi\n /opt/clang-p2996/bin/clang++ -std=c++2c -freflection -freflection-latest \\\n   -fexpansion-statements -stdlib=libc++ -Wl,-rpath,\"$P2996_LIBCXX_DIR\" \\\n   /tmp/refl-func.cpp -o /tmp/refl-clang \\\n@@ -639,6 +648,7 @@ def _tier3_var_lines(\n     *,\n     emulated: bool,\n     expected_llvm_version: str | None = None,\n+    gcc_latest: bool = True,\n ) -> str:\n     \"\"\"Injected-data header lines the tier-3 substrate reads.\n \n@@ -650,6 +660,11 @@ def _tier3_var_lines(\n     ``EXPECTED_LLVM_VERSION`` is the apt LLVM release (#294) the default-clang\n     and utility ``--version`` guards assert; empty leaves those guards dormant\n     (unit-test friendly), like the tier-1 guards.\n+    ``GCC_LATEST_PRESENT`` gates the kayari GCC-17 checks: that compiler is\n+    published for amd64 only, so demanding it on another architecture would\n+    fail the smoke over an artifact upstream never built (#698). It defaults to\n+    present — the direction that fails LOUDLY when a caller forgets to say,\n+    rather than silently dropping a real compiler check.\n     \"\"\"\n     strict = \"1\" if _SHA_RE.match(expected_p2996_ref) else \"\"\n     tsan_run_skip = \"1\" if emulated else \"\"\n@@ -657,6 +672,7 @@ def _tier3_var_lines(\n The local\n1897\t    devcontainer's base predates a branch's ``mise-system.toml`` pin bump (the\n1898\t    branch's base is built by its own PR CI, never locally), so the devcontainer\n1899\t    tier-3 version guard (injected by :func:`build_tier3_script` / ``smoke-script\n1900\t    --tier 3``) must expect the MERGE-BASE pin — reading HEAD would false-FAIL on\n1901\t    a branch that bumps the LLVM version. The\n1918\t    injected DATA is merge-base aware (the local base predates a branch's\n1919\t    image-input bump, which is validated by the branch's own PR CI build+smoke):\n1920\t\n1921\t    - **tier 1** — image identity + exact tool-set\n1922\t      (:func:`resolve_expected_identity_at_base` /\n1923\t      :func:`resolve_declared_tools_at_base`).\n1924\t    - **tier 3** — sanitizer + reflection compiler substrate\n1925\t      (:func:`resolve_expected_p2996_ref_at_base`). ``emulated=True`` is forced:\n1926\t      the amd64 container reports ``x86_64`` even under Rosetta on the arm64 Mac\n1927\t      dev host, so emulation is invisible from inside (``os.uname`` can't tell) —\n1928\t      and CI's native-runner smoke already exercises the TSan RUN, so the\n1929\t      devcontainer always skips it (the compile still runs, proving the\n1930\t      toolchain). asan/ubsan + reflection RUN fine under Rosetta and stay.\n1931\t\n1932\t    Tier 2 (pytest + mounts + doppler secrets) has NO python-generated core —\n1933\t    every tier-2 check is mount/env-dependent and has no CI no-mount counterpart,\n1934\t    so there is nothing to unify; it stays entirely bash. probe scheduled on it and asserted `uname -m` = aarch64 (#676).\n _RUNNER_LABELS = {\"amd64\": \"ubuntu-latest\", \"arm64\": \"ubuntu-24.04-arm\"}\n \n+# The architectures whose image carries the kayari `gcc-latest` trunk snapshot.\n+#\n+# NOT a subset waiting to be widened: upstream states the policy on its index\n+# page — \"Only the C and C++ compilers are included, and only for x86_64, in a\n+# single large package\" — so there is nothing to wait for. arm64's modern-GCC\n+# slot is conda-forge's `gcc` instead, which does publish linux-aarch64.\n+#\n+# The published image is therefore NOT architecture-symmetric, and pretending\n+# otherwise is what a smoke check would do if it asserted this compiler\n+# unconditionally: it would fail an arm64 build over an artifact that does not\n+# exist. clang-p2996 is unaffected — it is built from source (#698).\n+GCC_LATEST_ARCHES = (\"amd64\",)\n+\n+# mise's own spelling of each architecture — a THIRD name for the same axis,\n+# and the one both image lockfiles are keyed by. `uname -m` says x86_64, docker\n+# says amd64, mise says linux-x64; treating any two as interchangeable is how\n+# #698 shipped an arm64 image holding 131 x86_64 tool resolutions.\n+_MISE_LOCK_PLATFORM = {\"amd64\": \"linux-x64\", \"arm64\": \"linux-arm64\"}\n+\n+# The image's system-wide mise config. Its `[settings]` decide what the IMAGE\n+# resolves, which is a different question from what CI publishes — #698 is\n+# precisely the two answers disagreeing.\n+IMAGE_MISE_CONFIG = \".devcontainer/mise-system.toml\"\n+\n+_LOCKFILE_PLATFORMS_RE = re.compile(\n+    r\"^\\s*lockfile_platforms\\s*=\\s*\\[(?P<body>[^\\]]*)\\]\", re.MULTILINE\n+)\n+\n+# Anchored at line start so a COMMENTED pin (the honest record of why the line\n+# went away) does not re-trip the gate that removed it.\n+_PINNED_ARCH_RE = re.compile(\n+    r\"^\\s*arch\\s*=\\s*[\\\"'](?P<arch>[^\\\"']+)[\\\"']\", re.MULTILINE\n+)\n+\n \n @dataclass(frozen=True)\n class PublishTarget:\n@@ -216,6 +255,15 @@ def publish_matrix_main() -> int:\n     return 0\n \n \n+def ships_gcc_latest(platform: str) -> bool:\n+    \"\"\"Does an image built for ``platform`` carry the kayari gcc-latest deb?\n+\n+    Asked of the platform rather than answered by a boolean the caller worked\n+    out, so the asymmetry in :data:`GCC_LATEST_ARCHES` is stated once.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    return platform_arch(platform) in GCC_LATEST_ARCHES\n+\n+\n def normalize_arch(token: str) -> str | None:\n     \"\"\"Docker's architecture name for ``token``, or ``None`` if unrecognised.\"\"\"\n     return _ARCH_ALIASES.get(token.strip().lower())\n@@ -455,6 +503,93 @@ def find_unpublished_pin(repo_root: Path) -> str | None:\n     return None\n \n \n+def mise_lock_platforms() -> tuple[str, ...]:\n+    \"\"\"Mise's lock-platform name for every published architecture.\n+\n+    Raises ``ValueError`` naming the architecture rather than skipping it: a\n+    silently-dropped platform produces a lockfile that is complete by its own\n+    definition and wrong by the build's, which is the #698 failure exactly.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    platforms: list[str] = []\n+    for arch in PUBLISHED_ARCHES:\n+        platform = _MISE_LOCK_PLATFORM.get(arch)\n+        if platform is None:\n+            msg = (\n+                f\"architecture {arch!r} is declared in PUBLISHED_ARCHES but has \"\n+                f\"no mise lock-platform name — its tools would be resolved for \"\n+                f\"another architecture\"\n+            )\n+            raise ValueError(msg)\n+        platforms.append(platform)\n+    return tuple(platforms)\n+\n+\n+def _image_mise_config(repo_root: Path) -> str | None:\n+    try:\n+        return (repo_root / IMAGE_MISE_CONFIG).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    except OSError:\n+        return None\n+\n+\n+def find_lock_platform_drift(repo_root: Path) -> str | None:\n+    \"\"\"A message when the image locks fewer architectures than CI publishes.\n+\n+    ``lockfile_platforms`` scopes what ``mise lock`` writes, so an architecture\n+    absent from it gets **no lock entries at all** — and ``mise install\n+    --locked`` then resolves that architecture's tools from whatever platform is\n+    present. The build survives it, because the build-time self-checks count\n+    installed tools rather than executing them, so the first honest failure is a\n+    binary that will not run, hours later at smoke.\n+\n+    ``image_lock.py`` also reads this declaration to decide which platforms to\n+    regenerate, which is why a *missing* declaration is reported rather than\n+    read as \"all platforms\": deleting it narrows the refresh silently.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    text = _image_mise_config(repo_root)\n+    if text is None:\n+        return None\n+    match = _LOCKFILE_PLATFORMS_RE.search(text)\n+    if match is None:\n+        return (\n+            f\"{IMAGE_MISE_CONFIG} declares no `lockfile_platforms` — \"\n+            f\"`mise run lock-image` reads it to decide which platforms to \"\n+            f\"regenerate, so its absence narrows the locks instead of widening \"\n+            f\"them. Expected {list(mise_lock_platforms())}\"\n+        )\n+    declared = {token.strip().strip(\"\\\"'\") for token in match.group(\"body\").split(\",\")}\n+    missing = [p for p in mise_lock_platforms() if p not in declared]\n+    if missing:\n+        return (\n+            f\"{IMAGE_MISE_CONFIG} locks {sorted(declared - {''})} but the publish \"\n+            f\"matrix ships {list(PUBLISHED_ARCHES)} — {missing} would get no lock \"\n+            f\"entries, so those architectures resolve another platform's tools\"\n+        )\n+    return None\n+\n+\n+def find_pinned_image_arch(repo_root: Path) -> str | None:\n+    \"\"\"A message when the image config pins ``arch`` to a literal (#698 AC1).\n+\n+    Every matrix leg builds inside a container OF its target architecture, so\n+    mise's own detection already *is* the build target. A literal overrides that\n+    detection with whatever was true the day the line was written — and unlike a\n+    wrong ``--platform``, nothing downstream contradicts it: the image is built\n+    for one architecture and filled with another's binaries.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    text = _image_mise_config(repo_root)\n+    if text is None:\n+        return None\n+    match = _PINNED_ARCH_RE.search(text)\n+    if match is None:\n+        return None\n+    return (\n+        f'{IMAGE_MISE_CONFIG} pins `arch = \"{match.group(\"arch\")}\"` — the image '\n+        f\"would resolve that architecture's tools whatever it is being built for. \"\n+        f\"Delete the line: each matrix leg builds in a container of its own \"\n+        f\"architecture, so mise's detection is already the build target\"\n+    )\n+\n+\n #: What ``dotfiles-setup platform <field>`` can print. Shell callers (the\n #: `verify-arch` task) read these instead of re-implementing the triple→arch\n #: and triple→uname mappings in bash ([[zero-bash-logic]]).\n@@ -487,7 +622,13 @@ def platform_main(field: str) -> int:\n def platform_literals_main(repo_root: Path) -> int:\n     \"\"\"CLI entry: report re-appearing literals and default drift; exit 1 if any.\"\"\"\n     failed = False\n-    for message in (find_default_drift(repo_root), find_unpublished_pin(repo_root)):\n+    checks = (\n+        find_default_drift(repo_root),\n+        find_unpublished_pin(repo_root),\n+        find_lock_platform_drift(repo_root),\n+        find_pinned_image_arch(repo_root),\n+    )\n+    for message in checks:\n         if message is not None:\n             failed = True\n             logger.error(\"platform-literals FAIL: %s\", message)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"f64efb36-c4f5-488a-a1d6-a1cc64bf6c78","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:24:00.428Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"020d318c-976b-4818-8d04-8fd47c9e67b4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

That constraint pushes the set\n+  # somewhere better than YAML anyway: one list, unit-tested, with each\n+  # architecture's NATIVE runner label and per-architecture tag suffix attached.\n+  #\n+  # Every build job below fans out over this and exports `PLATFORM` for its leg.\n+  # bake reads that env var into its `PLATFORM` variable, and since #676 so do\n+  # the three content hashes — so a leg's build and its cache tags cannot\n+  # disagree about which architecture is being produced.\n+  # ============================================================\n+  plan:\n+    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n+    timeout-minutes: 10\n+    permissions:\n+      contents: read\n+    outputs:\n+      matrix: ${{ steps.matrix.outputs.matrix }}\n+    steps:\n+      - name: Checkout code\n+        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n+        with:\n+          ref: ${{ inputs.ref }}\n+          persist-credentials: false\n+      - name: Install mise (python + uv)\n+        uses: ./.github/actions/setup-mise\n+        with:\n+          install_args: \"python uv\"\n+      - name: Resolve the publish matrix\n+        id: matrix\n+        run: |\n+          set -euo pipefail\n+          matrix=$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup platform-matrix)\n+          echo \"matrix=${matrix}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n+          {\n+            echo \"## Publish plan\"\n+            echo \"\"\n+            echo '```json'\n+            echo \"${matrix}\"\n+            echo '```'\n+          } >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n+  # ============================================================\n   # Base cache prep: compute content-hash of devcontainer-base inputs\n   # (apt + mise install + cargo crates layer) and ensure\n   # ghcr.io/<owner>/<repo>:base-<hash16> exists. On hit, exits in <30s.\n@@ -89,17 +133,33 @@ jobs:\n   # base layer.\n   # ============================================================\n   base-prep:\n-    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n+    needs: [plan]\n+    strategy:\n+      # One architecture failing must not cancel the other: a partial result is\n+      # still diagnosable, and a cancelled leg reports as \"cancelled\" rather\n+      # than naming what broke.\n+      fail-fast: false\n+      matrix:\n+        target: ${{ fromJSON(needs.plan.outputs.matrix) }}\n+    runs-on: ${{ matrix.target.runner }}\n     # Cold base rebuild (apt + mise install + cargo crates + conda solve) runs\n     # ~20-30 min; conda solve time varies, so keep generous headroom.\n     timeout-minutes: 60\n     permissions:\n       contents: read\n       packages: write\n-    outputs:\n-      hash: ${{ steps.hash.outputs.hash }}\n-      cache_ref: ${{ steps.hash.outputs.cache_ref }}\n-      digest: ${{ steps.digest.outputs.digest }}\n+    # Read by BOTH `docker/bake-action` (into the `PLATFORM` HCL variable) and\n+    # `dotfiles-setup base-hash` (#676) — one value, so the image this leg\n+    # builds and the tag it is cached under cannot describe different\n+    # architectures.\n+    env:\n+      PLATFORM: ${{ matrix.target.platform }}\n+    # NOTE: deliberately NO job `outputs` any more. A matrix job has ONE output\n+    # set — the last leg to finish wins — so `needs.base-prep.outputs.cache_ref`\n+    # would hand the arm64 build the amd64 base image roughly half the time, and\n+    # the digest pin would then fail the solve with a mismatch that names\n+    # neither architecture. Downstream jobs recompute the hash for their own\n+    # leg instead; it is deterministic, so recomputing is free and unambiguous.\n     steps:\n       - name: Checkout code\n         uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n@@ -210,7 +270,17 @@ jobs:\n   # See .devcontainer/P2996-CACHE.md for the cache mechanism.\n   # ============================================================\n   p2996-prep:\n-    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n+    needs: [plan]\n+    strategy:\n+      fail-fast: false\n+      matrix:\n+        target: ${{ fromJSON(needs.plan.outputs.matrix) }}\n+    runs-on: ${{ matrix.target.runner }}\n+    # Native, never emulated: this job compiles clang-p2996 from source, and\n+    # under QEMU that is the multi-hour build made several times worse. The arm\n+    # runner label was measured, not assumed (#676).\n+    env:\n+      PLATFORM: ${{ matrix.target.platform }}\n     # Cold clang-p2996 build is a multi-hour from-source compile (~2h+). The\n     # earlier 60 was far too low and CANCELLED cold rebuilds (regression from\n     # #154's timeout-minutes addition; only warm cache-hits — seconds — masked\n@@ -220,10 +290,8 @@ jobs:\n     permissions:\n       contents: read\n       packages: write # explicit; do not rely on workflow-level inheritance\n-    outputs:\n-      hash: ${{ steps.hash.outputs.hash }}\n-      cache_ref: ${{ steps.hash.outputs.cache_ref }}\n-      digest: ${{ steps.digest.outputs.digest }}\n+    # No job `outputs` — see the note on base-prep: a matrix job has one output\n+    # set, so cross-leg reads silently mix architectures.\n     steps:\n       - name: Checkout code\n         uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n@@ -353,17 +421,23 @@ jobs:\n   # .github/workflows/AGENTS.md § three-tier probe cache.\n   # ============================================================\n   dev-prep:\n-    needs: [base-prep, p2996-prep]\n+    needs: [plan, base-prep, p2996-prep]\n     if: inputs.tag_strategy == 'pr'\n-    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n+    strategy:\n+      fail-fast: false\n+      matrix:\n+        target: ${{ fromJSON(needs.plan.outputs.matrix) }}\n+    runs-on: ${{ matrix.target.runner }}\n     timeout-minutes: 45\n     permissions:\n       contents: read\n       packages: write\n-    outputs:\n-      hash: ${{ steps.hash.outputs.hash }}\n-      cache_ref: ${{ steps.hash.outputs.cache_ref }}\n-      hit: ${{ steps.probe.outputs.hit }}\n+    env:\n+      PLATFORM: ${{ matrix.target.platform }}\n+    # `hit` is per-leg and CANNOT be a job output (last leg wins). {"parentUuid":"e8f7eb4e-2ddc-4838-a21e-12bd79fa0c9e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01B4e7vxUzEVYD5yVtbokTgy","type":"tool_result","content":"1\tname: dev-cache-probe\n2\tdescription: >-\n3\t  Probe the third-tier `:dev-<hash>` validated-image marker for ONE\n4\t  architecture, and report hit/miss plus the resolved cache ref.\n5\t\n6\t# Why this is a composite rather than a job output (#676).\n7\t#\n8\t# A GitHub matrix job has ONE `outputs` map, written by whichever leg finishes\n9\t# last — there is no per-leg output. So `needs.dev-prep.outputs.hit` under a\n10\t# two-architecture matrix reports one leg's answer to both, and the wrong half\n11\t# of the time that means building an image whose validated marker already\n12\t# existed, or skipping a build whose marker does not. Each leg therefore asks\n13\t# the registry itself, and the asking lives here so the three call sites cannot\n14\t# drift apart.\n15\t#\n16\t# The probe stays truthful after `build` runs: build pushes `:<sha>-<arch>`,\n17\t# while `:dev-<hash>` is stamped only by `dev-tag`, only after smoke passes. So\n18\t# at every call site a hit means \"this exact content was already built AND\n19\t# validated\", never \"we just built it\".\n20\t#\n21\t# REQUIRES the caller to have run `actions/checkout` and `./.github/actions/\n22\t# setup-mise` (python + uv) first, and to have exported `PLATFORM` for the leg —\n23\t# `dev-hash` resolves it exactly as bake does.\n24\t\n25\tinputs:\n26\t  image:\n27\t    description: \"Registry image name without a tag (ghcr.io/owner/name)\"\n28\t    required: true\n29\t\n30\toutputs:\n31\t  hash:\n32\t    description: \"The dev content hash for this architecture\"\n33\t    value: ${{ steps.probe.outputs.hash }}\n34\t  cache_ref:\n35\t    description: \"The fully-qualified :dev-<hash> marker ref\"\n36\t    value: ${{ steps.probe.outputs.cache_ref }}\n37\t  hit:\n38\t    description: \"'true' when the validated marker already exists\"\n39\t    value: ${{ steps.probe.outputs.hit }}\n40\t\n41\truns:\n42\t  using: composite\n43\t  steps:\n44\t    - name: Compute dev hash and probe the validated marker\n45\t      id: probe\n46\t      shell: bash\n47\t      env:\n48\t        IMAGE: ${{ inputs.image }}\n49\t      run: |\n50\t        set -euo pipefail\n51\t        hash=$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup dev-hash)\n52\t        cache_ref=\"${IMAGE}:dev-${hash}\"\n53\t        {\n54\t          echo \"hash=${hash}\"\n55\t          echo \"cache_ref=${cache_ref}\"\n56\t        } >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n57\t        # Discriminate a legitimate \"manifest not found\" MISS from an\n58\t        # auth/network/registry failure. A blanket `|| hit=false` would turn a\n59\t        # broken token into a silent full rebuild on every run — expensive, and\n60\t        # it looks exactly like a cache that never warms. Capturing stderr\n61\t        # rather than `2>/dev/null` honors `build.no-stderr-suppression`.\n62\t        if out=$(docker manifest inspect \"${cache_ref}\" 2>&1); then\n63\t          echo \"hit=true\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n64\t          echo \"Dev cache HIT for ${PLATFORM}: ${cache_ref}\" >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n65\t        else\n66\t          rc=$?\n67\t          if printf '%s\\n' \"$out\" | grep -qiE 'manifest unknown|not found|no such manifest'; then\n68\t            echo \"hit=false\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n69\t            echo \"Dev cache MISS for ${PLATFORM}: ${cache_ref}\" >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n70\t          else\n71\t            echo \"FAIL: docker manifest inspect failed with non-miss error (rc=$rc):\" >&2\n72\t            printf '%s\\n' \"$out\" >&2\n73\t            exit \"$rc\"\n74\t          fi\n75\t        fi\n76\t"}]},"uuid":"720b5a3d-a24f-4f91-8108-7acadf23d383","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:24:24.361Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e8f7eb4e-2ddc-4838-a21e-12bd79fa0c9e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

The tag set is now written by `--set dev.tags`\n+        # below: every matrix leg pushes ONE per-architecture tag, and the\n+        # moving tags (:sha, :pr-NNN, :dev, :latest) are an index the\n+        # `manifest` job assembles from them. The prior\n       # hand-rolled \"Build performance summary\" + \"Build diagnostics\" steps\n       # duplicated this and have been removed.\n       - name: Export build outputs\n         id: out\n-        # Surface the canonical :<short-sha> ref (the one smoke validates and\n-        # promote retags on merge) and the dev image digest for callers/Phase\n-        # C. It would raise on the\n+        #     first iteration, every run.\n+        #   * Its payload embeds the requested `platform`, so the reports differ\n+        #     BY CONSTRUCTION and the \"they must differ\" branch is unreachable —\n+        #     a check that can only pass\n+        #     (`.claude/rules/probes-need-a-control-arm.md`).\n+        #\n+        # So compare the thing that is actually arch-derived: the digest each\n+        # index entry points at, read straight from the published document.\n+        # Distinct digests, and each per-architecture TAG resolving to the same\n+        # digest as its index entry, is exactly AC2 — and it genuinely fails if\n+        # the index ever points two entries at one image.\n+        env:\n+          IMAGE: ${{ env.CONTAINER_REGISTRY }}/${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}\n+          IMAGE_REF: ${{ steps.merge.outputs.image_ref }}\n+          MATRIX: ${{ needs.plan.outputs.matrix }}\n+          COMMIT_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}\n+        run: |\n+          set -euo pipefail\n+          short_sha=$(printf '%s' \"${COMMIT_SHA}\" | head -c 7)\n+          raw=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${IMAGE_REF}\" --raw)\n+          digests=()\n+          while read -r arch suffix; do\n+            entry=$(printf '%s' \"${raw}\" | jq -r \\\n+              --arg a \"${arch}\" \\\n+              '.manifests[] | select(.platform.architecture == $a and .platform.os != \"unknown\") | .digest')\n+            if [ -z \"${entry}\" ]; then\n+              echo \"FAIL: no ${arch} entry in ${IMAGE_REF}\" >&2\n+              exit 1\n+            fi\n+            tagged=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${IMAGE}:${short_sha}-${suffix}\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}')\n+            if [ \"${entry}\" != \"${tagged}\" ]; then\n+              echo \"FAIL: :${short_sha}-${suffix} is ${tagged} but the index's ${arch} entry is ${entry}\" >&2\n+              exit 1\n+            fi\n+            echo \"${arch}: ${entry}\"\n+            digests+=(\"${entry}\")\n+          done < <(printf '%s' \"${MATRIX}\" | jq -r '.[] | \"\\(.arch) \\(.tag_suffix)\"')\n+          # Two entries sharing a digest mean the index is single-architecture\n+          # wearing two labels — which every \"did the pull succeed\" check passes.\n+          unique=$(printf '%s\\n' \"${digests[@]}\" | sort -u | wc -l)\n+          if [ \"${unique}\" -ne \"${#digests[@]}\" ]; then\n+            echo \"FAIL: index entries share a digest — not really multi-architecture\" >&2\n+            exit 1\n+          fi\n+          echo \"OK: each architecture resolves to a distinct image\" >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"2b5dc7ec-79cd-465d-be9c-ade8f73e2ea0","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:24:44.158Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7b7afc3f-2658-41c2-9368-ef4e033e1d28","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"b8f70e1d-53f6-41a2-8d42-f5e5fb767ec0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018doSBbGfHHTV5LQfYczxZH","type":"tool_result","content":"diff --git a/.github/workflows/ci.yml b/.github/workflows/ci.yml\nindex e30ec63..104f64e 100644\n--- a/.github/workflows/ci.yml\n+++ b/.github/workflows/ci.yml\n@@ -493,6 +493,31 @@ jobs:\n             echo \"  :latest: ${latest_digest}\" >&2\n             exit 1\n           fi\n+          # The moving PER-ARCHITECTURE tags (#676). `:dev` is a multi-\n+          # architecture index, so a caller that must name one architecture —\n+          # a cross-architecture identity check, a `mise.local.toml` pin —\n+          # reaches for `:dev-<arch>`. Advancing `:dev` without advancing those\n+          # leaves a stale image behind a moving name, which is exactly the\n+          # confusing failure mode the spec's D1 warns about, and it is silent:\n+          # the pin keeps resolving, just to last month's image.\n+          #\n+          # The architecture list is read from the INDEX rather than passed in,\n+          # so it describes what was actually published and cannot drift from\n+          # the matrix that produced it. Attestation entries carry\n+          # `os: unknown` and are filtered out.\n+          raw=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${SOURCE_REF}\" --raw)\n+          while read -r arch digest; do\n+            [ -n \"${arch}\" ] || continue\n+            docker buildx imagetools create \\\n+              --tag \"${IMAGE}:dev-${arch}\" \"${IMAGE}@${digest}\"\n+            got=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${IMAGE}:dev-${arch}\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}')\n+            if [ \"${got}\" != \"${digest}\" ]; then\n+              echo \"FAIL: :dev-${arch} is ${got}, expected ${digest}\" >&2\n+              exit 1\n+            fi\n+            echo \"- Per-arch tag: \\`:dev-${arch}\\` → \\`${digest}\\`\" >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n+          done < <(printf '%s' \"${raw}\" | jq -r \\\n+            '.manifests[] | select(.platform.os != \"unknown\") | \"\\(.platform.architecture) \\(.digest)\"')\n           {\n             echo \"## Promote (tag-only)\"\n             echo \"\"\ndiff --git a/docker-bake.hcl b/docker-bake.hcl\nindex 8e6ceac..a78cbb1 100644\n--- a/docker-bake.hcl\n+++ b/docker-bake.hcl\n@@ -130,11 +130,22 @@ target \"dev\" {\n     CLANG_P2996_REF = CLANG_P2996_REF\n   }\n   # Tags inherited from docker-metadata-action (CI overrides with SHA/latest/PR tags)\n+  #\n+  # The scope is SUFFIXED by the architecture (#676). It used to be the bare\n+  # `dotfiles-dev` — one scope for every build — which was harmless while only\n+  # one architecture was ever built, and is a correctness bug the moment two\n+  # are: `type=gha` is content-addressed *within a scope*, so two matrix legs\n+  # sharing one would race to overwrite each other's index and each would\n+  # repeatedly evict the other's layers. Deriving the suffix from the same\n+  # PLATFORM variable that selects the build keeps the two in lockstep by\n+  # construction, and adds no literal for `no_platform_literals` to reject.\n+  # Probed both arms: unset => `dotfiles-dev-linux-amd64-v2`; PLATFORM exported\n+  # as the arm triple => `dotfiles-dev-linux-arm64-v8`.\n   cache-from = [\n-    \"type=gha,scope=dotfiles-dev\",\n+    \"type=gha,scope=dotfiles-dev-${replace(PLATFORM, \"/\", \"-\")}\",\n   ]\n   cache-to = [\n-    \"type=gha,scope=dotfiles-dev,mode=max\",\n+    \"type=gha,scope=dotfiles-dev-${replace(PLATFORM, \"/\", \"-\")},mode=max\",\n   ]\n   # mode=max records the full build graph (materials, args, steps) so the\n   # published provenance can answer \"what exactly went into this image\"","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"5d3170c3-862a-494b-b515-518cb3687113","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:25:02.064Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b8f70e1d-53f6-41a2-8d42-f5e5fb767ec0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"93462d0d-534c-4ef4-801a-93c1653f1325","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdtbBWoFL3DYXNB7p23oV","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_015mBUPN5t61rYGkfyGfXFpC","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"sed -n '430,540p' .github/workflows/ci.yml","description":"Read promote job in ci.yml"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2812,"cache_read_input_tokens":148584,"output_tokens":2098,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2812},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2098,"cache_read_input_tokens":148584,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2812,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2812,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdtbBUhjysFtWGTWH95i3","attributionSkill":"code-review","type":"assistant","uuid":"fd4df06f-c36c-42be-a2e7-fc901105c716","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:25:29.523Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}
{"parentUuid":"fd4df06f-c36c-42be-a2e7-fc901105c716","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015mBUPN5t61rYGkfyGfXFpC","type":"tool_result","content":"          registry: ${{ env.CONTAINER_REGISTRY }}\n          username: ${{ github.actor }}\n          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n      - name: Probe source PR image exists (else fall through to rebuild)\n        if: steps.pr.outputs.fallback != 'true'\n        id: source_probe\n        env:\n          IMAGE: ${{ env.CONTAINER_REGISTRY }}/${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}\n          PR_NUMBER: ${{ steps.pr.outputs.pr_number }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          source_ref=\"${IMAGE}:pr-${PR_NUMBER}\"\n          # The PR image may have been pruned (GHCR retention, manual\n          # delete) by the time a long-running revert merges. If so, the\n          # promote retag would fail hard; we instead route this to the\n          # rebuild fallback path so the operator never gets stuck. We\n          # also discriminate auth/network/registry errors from the\n          # legitimate \"tag not found\" miss.\n          err_log=$(mktemp)\n          if source_digest=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${source_ref}\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}' 2>\"${err_log}\"); then\n            if [ -z \"${source_digest}\" ]; then\n              echo \"FAIL: imagetools inspect succeeded but Manifest.Digest was empty for ${source_ref}\" >&2\n              cat \"${err_log}\" >&2\n              exit 1\n            fi\n            {\n              echo \"source_present=true\"\n              echo \"source_ref=${source_ref}\"\n              echo \"source_digest=${source_digest}\"\n            } >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n          elif grep -qiE 'manifest unknown|not found|no such manifest' \"${err_log}\"; then\n            echo \"WARN: ${source_ref} not present (retention prune?); switching to rebuild fallback\" >&2\n            echo \"source_present=false\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n          else\n            echo \"FAIL: imagetools inspect failed with non-miss error:\" >&2\n            cat \"${err_log}\" >&2\n            exit 1\n          fi\n      - name: Retag PR image as :dev and :latest\n        if: steps.pr.outputs.fallback != 'true' && steps.source_probe.outputs.source_present == 'true'\n        env:\n          IMAGE: ${{ env.CONTAINER_REGISTRY }}/${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}\n          SOURCE_REF: ${{ steps.source_probe.outputs.source_ref }}\n          SOURCE_DIGEST: ${{ steps.source_probe.outputs.source_digest }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          docker buildx imagetools create \\\n            --tag \"${IMAGE}:dev\" \\\n            --tag \"${IMAGE}:latest\" \\\n            \"${SOURCE_REF}\"\n          # Verify both new tags resolve to the source digest. Without\n          # this, `imagetools create` could no-op silently (concurrent\n          # push race) and pre-existing stale-but-equal digests would\n          # pass the dev==latest check while neither tag was updated.\n          dev_digest=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${IMAGE}:dev\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}')\n          latest_digest=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${IMAGE}:latest\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}')\n          for d in \"${dev_digest}\" \"${latest_digest}\"; do\n            [ -n \"$d\" ] || { echo \"FAIL: empty digest after retag\" >&2; exit 1; }\n          done\n          if [ \"${dev_digest}\" != \"${SOURCE_DIGEST}\" ] || [ \"${latest_digest}\" != \"${SOURCE_DIGEST}\" ]; then\n            echo \"FAIL: retag did not propagate source digest\" >&2\n            echo \"  source : ${SOURCE_DIGEST}\" >&2\n            echo \"  :dev   : ${dev_digest}\"   >&2\n            echo \"  :latest: ${latest_digest}\" >&2\n            exit 1\n          fi\n          # The moving PER-ARCHITECTURE tags (#676). `:dev` is a multi-\n          # architecture index, so a caller that must name one architecture —\n          # a cross-architecture identity check, a `mise.local.toml` pin —\n          # reaches for `:dev-<arch>`. Advancing `:dev` without advancing those\n          # leaves a stale image behind a moving name, which is exactly the\n          # confusing failure mode the spec's D1 warns about, and it is silent:\n          # the pin keeps resolving, just to last month's image.\n          #\n          # The architecture list is read from the INDEX rather than passed in,\n          # so it describes what was actually published and cannot drift from\n          # the matrix that produced it. Attestation entries carry\n          # `os: unknown` and are filtered out.\n          raw=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${SOURCE_REF}\" --raw)\n          while read -r arch digest; do\n            [ -n \"${arch}\" ] || continue\n            docker buildx imagetools create \\\n              --tag \"${IMAGE}:dev-${arch}\" \"${IMAGE}@${digest}\"\n            got=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${IMAGE}:dev-${arch}\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}')\n            if [ \"${got}\" != \"${digest}\" ]; then\n              echo \"FAIL: :dev-${arch} is ${got}, expected ${digest}\" >&2\n              exit 1\n            fi\n            echo \"- Per-arch tag: \\`:dev-${arch}\\` → \\`${digest}\\`\" >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n          done < <(printf '%s' \"${raw}\" | jq -r \\\n            '.manifests[] | select(.platform.os != \"unknown\") | \"\\(.platform.architecture) \\(.digest)\"')\n          {\n            echo \"## Promote (tag-only)\"\n            echo \"\"\n            echo \"- Source: \\`${SOURCE_REF}\\`\"\n            echo \"- New tags: \\`:dev\\`, \\`:latest\\` (digest \\`${SOURCE_DIGEST}\\`)\"\n          } >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n      - name: Trigger fallback rebuild\n        if: steps.pr.outputs.fallback == 'true' || steps.source_probe.outputs.source_present == 'false'\n        env:\n          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          # Verify the dispatch actually created a run — `gh workflow run`\n          # returns 0 on a successful queue but does not guarantee a run\n          # appears (rare API hiccup, scope misconfig, branch protection).\n          # Without verification, a green \"fallback rebuild\" summary could\n          # mask a missing :dev/:latest publication.\n          gh workflow run ci.yml \\\n            --ref main \\\n            -f target=dev \\","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"ca4d4c03-eace-4bae-9b18-157f002af06d","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:25:29.821Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fd4df06f-c36c-42be-a2e7-fc901105c716","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}
