Deliberately matches\n# the arch words too (`linux/amd64` without a level), because a site that drops\n# the level is exactly the split-brain this gate exists to catch.\n_LITERAL_RE = re.compile(r\"linux/(?:amd64|arm64|x86_64|aarch64)(?:/v\\d+)?\")\n\n# Files scanned for a re-appearing literal. Prose and archived evidence are out\n# of scope — a research report records what was true when it was written, and\n# rewriting it would falsify the record (`agent-artifact-conventions.md`).\n_SCANNED_SUFFIXES = (\".py\", \".toml\", \".hcl\", \".json\", \".sh\", \".yml\", \".yaml\")\n\n# Tests legitimately name concrete platforms: a fixture asserting that another\n# architecture bumps a content hash cannot express itself through the resolver\n# it is testing without becoming tautological (`tests/AGENTS.md`).\n_SCAN_EXCLUDED_PREFIXES = (\n    \"docs/\",\n    \"missions/\",\n    \"plugins/\",\n    \"tests/\",\n)\n\n# This module is the definition site: it composes every triple from\n# `_MICROARCH_LEVEL` and never hard-codes one, but it must be able to NAME them\n# — in its docstring, in the scan pattern, and in the explanation of why a\n# triple is not an arch word. Documentation that cannot use the example is\n# worse documentation, and this is the one file a reader comes to for the\n# answer. Nothing here issues a `--platform`, so the exemption costs no\n# coverage.\n_SCAN_EXCLUDED_PATHS = (\"python/src/dotfiles_setup/platform_target.py\",)\n\n\n# The architectures the published image ships as one manifest (#676), in the\n# order the CI matrix runs them.\nPUBLISHED_ARCHES = (\"amd64\", \"arm64\")\n\n# The GitHub-hosted runner label that executes each architecture NATIVELY.\n# Native is the whole ruling: a single bake emitting both platforms would build\n# the arm64 half under QEMU, and that half compiles GCC 16.2 and clang-p2996 —\n# the \"~2h compiler\" rebuild `docker-bake.hcl` already names, paid on emulated\n# CPU. Availability of the arm label is measured, not assumed: a control-armed\n# probe scheduled on it and asserted `uname -m` = aarch64 (#676).\n_RUNNER_LABELS = {\"amd64\": \"ubuntu-latest\", \"arm64\": \"ubuntu-24.04-arm\"}\n\n# The architectures whose image carries the kayari `gcc-latest` trunk snapshot.\n#\n# NOT a subset waiting to be widened: upstream states the policy on its index\n# page — \"Only the C and C++ compilers are included, and only for x86_64, in a\n# single large package\" — so there is nothing to wait for. arm64's modern-GCC\n# slot is conda-forge's `gcc` instead, which does publish linux-aarch64.\n#\n# The published image is therefore NOT architecture-symmetric, and pretending\n# otherwise is what a smoke check would do if it asserted this compiler\n# unconditionally: it would fail an arm64 build over an artifact that does not\n# exist. clang-p2996 is unaffected — it is built from source (#698).\nGCC_LATEST_ARCHES = (\"amd64\",)\n\n# mise's own spelling of each architecture — a THIRD name for the same axis,\n# and the one both image lockfiles are keyed by. `uname -m` says x86_64, docker\n# says amd64, mise says linux-x64; treating any two as interchangeable is how\n# #698 shipped an arm64 image holding 131 x86_64 tool resolutions.\n_MISE_LOCK_PLATFORM = {\"amd64\": \"linux-x64\", \"arm64\": \"linux-arm64\"}\n\n# The image's system-wide mise config. Its `[settings]` decide what the IMAGE\n# resolves, which is a different question from what CI publishes — #698 is\n# precisely the two answers disagreeing.\nIMAGE_MISE_CONFIG = \".devcontainer/mise-system.toml\"\n\n_LOCKFILE_PLATFORMS_RE = re.compile(\n    r\"^\\s*lockfile_platforms\\s*=\\s*\\[(?P<body>[^\\]]*)\\]\", re.MULTILINE\n)\n\n# Anchored at line start so a COMMENTED pin (the honest record of why the line\n# went away) does not re-trip the gate that removed it.\n_PINNED_ARCH_RE = re.compile(\n    r\"^\\s*arch\\s*=\\s*[\\\"'](?P<arch>[^\\\"']+)[\\\"']\", re.MULTILINE\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass PublishTarget:\n    \"\"\"One architecture of the published image, fully resolved for CI.\n\n    ``tag_suffix`` is what turns the moving tag into a per-architecture one\n    (``:<sha>`` → ``:<sha>-arm64``). Every matrix leg must push a DISTINCT tag:","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"c7ec57ad-0631-48df-9e16-93b086281e7e","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:25:40.302Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d444ee91-15aa-49cd-99ee-4dc930671ab4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

Every matrix leg must push a DISTINCT tag:\n    they run concurrently against one registry, so two legs sharing a tag do not\n    merge, they overwrite — and the loser's smoke result silently describes an\n    image nobody can pull any more.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    platform: str\n    arch: str\n    runner: str\n    tag_suffix: str\n\n\ndef _publish_target(arch: str) -> PublishTarget:\n    \"\"\"Resolve one architecture, or raise naming what is unwired.\n\n    Raises ``ValueError`` rather than defaulting a missing runner label to\n    something plausible: an empty ``runs-on`` queues the job against no runner\n    at all, so it hangs to the job timeout and reads as GitHub capacity rather\n    than as this table being incomplete.\n    \"\"\"\n    level = _MICROARCH_LEVEL.get(arch)\n    runner = _RUNNER_LABELS.get(arch)\n    if level is None or runner is None:\n        missing = \"microarchitecture level\" if level is None else \"runner label\"\n        msg = (\n            f\"architecture {arch!r} is declared in PUBLISHED_ARCHES but has no \"\n            f\"{missing} — publishing it would emit an unbuildable matrix entry\"\n        )\n        raise ValueError(msg)\n    return PublishTarget(\n        platform=f\"linux/{arch}/{level}\",\n        arch=arch,\n        runner=runner,\n        tag_suffix=arch,\n    )\n\n\ndef published_targets() -> tuple[PublishTarget, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Every architecture the image publishes, in matrix order.\"\"\"\n    return tuple(_publish_target(arch) for arch in PUBLISHED_ARCHES)\n\n\ndef publish_matrix_json() -> str:\n    \"\"\"The publish matrix as one line of JSON, for a workflow ``fromJSON``.\n\n    One line because ``>> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"`` is line-oriented — a pretty-printed\n    payload is read as a truncated first line and the matrix silently loses\n    every architecture after the first.\n    \"\"\"\n    return json.dumps([asdict(target) for target in published_targets()])\n\n\ndef publish_matrix_main() -> int:\n    \"\"\"CLI entry: print the publish matrix for the CI job that fans out.\"\"\"\n    sys.stdout.write(f\"{publish_matrix_json()}\\n\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef ships_gcc_latest(platform: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Does an image built for ``platform`` carry the kayari gcc-latest deb?\n\n    Asked of the platform rather than answered by a boolean the caller worked\n    out, so the asymmetry in :data:`GCC_LATEST_ARCHES` is stated once.\n    \"\"\"\n    return platform_arch(platform) in GCC_LATEST_ARCHES\n\n\ndef normalize_arch(token: str) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Docker's architecture name {"parentUuid":"9fda21d5-8747-4d12-8953-9eddf387efb5","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Wxp1mcd8iQZt7r936hKLM6","type":"tool_result","content":"# --prefer-index Behavior\n\n**Default:** `true`\n\n**What it does:** According to the documentation, when `--prefer-index` is enabled (the default), the command \"prefer[s] outputting an image index or manifest list instead of performing a carbon copy.\"\n\n**Single source behavior:** When you provide a single source that is *not* a manifest list or index, the output will be \"a manifest list\" by default. This means a new index is created wrapping the source manifest, so the resulting digest will differ from the source. \n\nIf you want to preserve the original manifest format instead, you can disable this with `--prefer-index=false`, which \"attempts to preserve the source manifest format in the output.\""}]},"uuid":"7c746869-5e6f-4a49-b943-71b376acfe5d","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:26:00.624Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9fda21d5-8747-4d12-8953-9eddf387efb5","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"05a43faf-f91e-45d3-8e60-8e2e156e5e3f","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdtbHVdy2w9Rf2MCjvhF4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_0116wq91hKmuH4dYXL9et6wN","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"git show a126a64 -- python/verification/suites.toml | cat","description":"Show suites.toml diff for 698"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":8183,"cache_read_input_tokens":160993,"output_tokens":1961,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":8183},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1961,"cache_read_input_tokens":160993,"cache_creation_input_tokens":8183,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":8183,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdtbHSkaWRyJ7WKX1HjyL","attributionSkill":"code-review","type":"assistant","uuid":"7b12e461-acbc-402d-a736-d137dce4b43a","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:26:52.958Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}
{"parentUuid":"7b12e461-acbc-402d-a736-d137dce4b43a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0116wq91hKmuH4dYXL9et6wN","type":"tool_result","content":"commit a126a64d6af51eb03f28324a067d4465879a88d2\nAuthor: Raymond Manaloto <ray.manaloto@gmail.com>\nDate:   Mon Aug 10 01:22:14 2026 -0500\n\n    fix(image): build each architecture with its own tools and packages (#698)\n    \n    #676 published an arm64 image; its CONTENTS were still amd64. Three\n    independent causes, each of which survived the build because every\n    build-time self-check counted or stat'd rather than executed.\n    \n    1. `mise-system.toml` pinned `arch = \"x86_64\"`, so any image resolved\n       x86_64 tools regardless of its build target. Deleted: each matrix leg\n       builds inside a container of its own architecture, so mise's native\n       detection already IS the target and a literal can only disagree with\n       it. `lockfile_platforms` now covers every published architecture, and\n       both image locks were regenerated in-container — system 0 -> 132\n       linux-arm64 entries, runtime 0 -> 35, tool counts unchanged (49/22)\n       and macos-x64 coverage preserved.\n    \n    2. Two hand-downloaded single-architecture `.deb`s. Probed upstream\n       before implementing, per the ticket's first AC: graphviz publishes no\n       Linux arm64 deb (44 files in the 15.1.0 release; its only arm64\n       artefact is a macOS .pkg, which is also the control arm proving the\n       negative is real), and gcc-latest publishes none BY POLICY — the\n       maintainer states \"only for x86_64\" on the index page. So graphviz\n       falls back to apt on other architectures, and arm64 ships without\n       gcc-latest. Ray's call, 2026-08-10, recorded as D31 in the spec: the\n       image is deliberately NOT architecture-symmetric, arm64's modern-GCC\n       slot goes to conda-forge gcc (linux-aarch64: 204 files at 16.1.0,\n       identical coverage to linux-64), and \"exactly 3 gcc compilers\"\n       becomes an arch-dependent invariant.\n    \n    3. Nothing executed a mise-installed binary. A wrong-architecture tool\n       downloads, installs, lists and stats exactly like a correct one; the\n       only thing it cannot do is run. `ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` runs six of\n       them, spanning backends because the backend is what resolves the\n       platform. It asserts the capability rather than sniffing a symptom\n       (probes-need-a-control-arm rule 9) — no uname compare, no ELF read,\n       no version string, each of which would bind something upstream owns.\n    \n    Gates: `find_lock_platform_drift` + `find_pinned_image_arch` wired into\n    the existing platform-literals CLI (both FAILED on the tree before the\n    fix); `platforms_to_lock` unions the committed lock with what the\n    publish matrix owes, closing #698's ordering trap where the regen meant\n    to add an architecture would skip it for having no entries yet; contract\n    `build.image-architecture-integrity` binds all nine wiring sites.\n    \n    `build_smoke_script` grew past ruff's PLR0913 threshold, which was a\n    fair signal — `emulated` and `gcc_latest` are both facts about one\n    architecture arriving from one platform triple, so they are now an\n    `ImageArch` value with a `for_platform` constructor.\n    \n    Verified: lint rc=0, pytest 1998 passed, verify 129 passed / 0 failed,\n    pin-actions rc=0. arm64 apt pins probed with both arms — real pins PASS,\n    an injected bogus version FAILs. Mutation-tested the TARGETARCH gate:\n    deleting one `ARG TARGETARCH` fails exactly one test.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01MCFymQ3miUyFFWvQ6fouQm\n\ndiff --git a/python/verification/suites.toml b/python/verification/suites.toml\nindex 1051241..140c24f 100644\n--- a/python/verification/suites.toml\n+++ b/python/verification/suites.toml\n@@ -337,7 +337,7 @@ per_path_tokens = { \".devcontainer/Dockerfile\" = [\n \n [[suite]]\n name = \"build.gcc-latest-reflection\"\n-description = \"GCC-16 with P2996 reflection must be installed via precompiled .deb\"\n+description = \"GCC-17 with P2996 reflection must be installed via precompiled .deb — and, since #698, ONLY on the architectures that have one. kayari.org publishes the gcc-latest trunk snapshot for x86_64 alone and says so as policy on its index page ('only for x86_64'), so an ungated download fails the arm64 base build outright. Both the install and its reflection smoke therefore carry a TARGETARCH branch, and each branch's skip message is bound here: deleting a branch is the realistic regression, and it would otherwise surface only as a 30-minute base build failing on a .deb that cannot exist. The `-freflection` token pins the smoke's own compile line (1x in the file; bare `-freflection` matches 5x and `/tmp/refl-gcc.cpp` 3x, so neither alone binds a site).\"\n category = \"build\"\n check_type = \"static\"\n handler = \"require_tokens\"\n@@ -345,7 +345,51 @@ paths = [\".devcontainer/Dockerfile\"]\n per_path_tokens = { \".devcontainer/Dockerfile\" = [\n     \"gcc-latest.deb\",\n     \"test -x /opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++\",\n-    \"-freflection \\\\\\n      /tmp/refl-gcc.cpp\",\n+    \"-freflection \\\\\\n        /tmp/refl-gcc.cpp\",\n+    \"gcc-latest: SKIPPED on \",\n+    \"gcc-latest reflection smoke: SKIPPED on \",\n+] }\n+\n+[[suite]]\n+name = \"build.image-architecture-integrity\"\n+description = \"#698: publishing an architecture is not the same as BUILDING one. #676 shipped an arm64 image whose mise config pinned `arch = \\\"x86_64\\\"`, so 131 tool entries resolved for the wrong CPU — and the build never noticed, because every build-time self-check COUNTS or STATS (`mise ls --installed | wc -l`, `test -d`, a non-empty shims dir) and a wrong-architecture binary is present, executable by its mode bits, and counted exactly like a correct one. The only thing it cannot do is run, so `ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` + its loop RUN it; the failure message is bound rather than the loop body because deleting the invocation and keeping a `test -x` is the regression that reproduces the original defect. {"parentUuid":"6c7c52b9-d847-40b4-afc6-731c7ab71069","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014Bt2AMYz5TTCK7gHMDPztg","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Copyright (c) 2026 Raymond Manaloto\n2\t\"\"\"The image's own architecture handling (#698).\n3\t\n4\t#676 published an arm64 image; #698 is the discovery that the *contents* were\n5\tstill amd64. Two independent causes, and this module pins both:\n6\t\n7\t1. **mise resolved x86_64 tools** into whatever it was building, because\n8\t   ``mise-system.toml`` pinned ``arch``. Covered by ``test_platform_target.py``;\n9\t   what is covered here is the build-time consequence — the existing self-checks\n10\t   COUNT installed tools (``mise ls --installed | wc -l``), and a count cannot\n11\t   see a binary that will not run. Only executing one can.\n12\t\n13\t2. **Two hand-downloaded ``.deb``s are single-architecture.** graphviz publishes\n14\t   no Linux arm64 build and ``gcc-latest`` publishes none by upstream policy\n15\t   (\"only for x86_64\"), so both downloads must be gated on ``TARGETARCH``.\n16\t\n17\tThe sharpest trap here is BuildKit's: ``TARGETARCH`` is an *automatic* build\n18\targ, and an automatic arg that a stage does not declare expands to the **empty\n19\tstring** rather than erroring. A stage that branches on an undeclared\n20\t``TARGETARCH`` therefore takes the else-branch on EVERY architecture — so the\n21\tamd64 image would silently lose the very packages this gating exists to keep,\n22\tand nothing in the build would say so.\n23\t\"\"\"\n24\t\n25\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n26\t\n27\timport re\n28\timport sys\n29\tfrom pathlib import Path\n30\t\n31\tsys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent.parent / \"python\" / \"src\"))\n32\t\n33\tfrom dotfiles_setup import image, platform_target\n34\t\n35\tREPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).parent.parent\n36\tDOCKERFILE = REPO_ROOT / \".devcontainer\" / \"Dockerfile\"\n37\tSHARED_FRAGMENT = REPO_ROOT / \".config\" / \"mise\" / \"conf.d\" / \"shared.toml\"\n38\t\n39\t#: The build args whose payloads only exist for amd64.\n40\t_SINGLE_ARCH_DOWNLOADS = (\"GRAPHVIZ_DEB\", \"GCC_LATEST_DEB\")\n41\t\n42\t_STAGE_RE = re.compile(r\"^FROM\\s+.*?\\s+AS\\s+(?P<name>\\S+)\", re.IGNORECASE)\n43\t_PROBES_RE = re.compile(r'^ARG\\s+ARCH_EXEC_PROBES=\"(?P<names>[^\"]+)\"', re.MULTILINE)\n44\t\n45\t\n46\tdef _dockerfile() -> str:\n47\t    return DOCKERFILE.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n48\t\n49\t\n50\tdef _stages(text: str) -> dict[str, str]:\n51\t    \"\"\"The Dockerfile split into stage bodies, keyed by stage name.\n52\t\n53\t    Build args do not cross a ``FROM``, which is exactly why an undeclared\n54\t    automatic arg is a live hazard rather than a style point — so the unit this\n55\t    module reasons about has to be the stage, not the file.\n56\t    \"\"\"\n57\t    stages: dict[str, list[str]] = {}\n58\t    current: list[str] | None = None\n59\t    for line in text.splitlines():\n60\t        match = _STAGE_RE.match(line)\n61\t        if match is not None:\n62\t            current = stages.setdefault(match.group(\"name\"), [])\n63\t            continue\n64\t        if current is not None:\n65\t            current.append(line)\n66\t    return {name: \"\\n\".join(body) for name, body in stages.items()}\n67\t\n68\t\n69\tdef _declared_tools() -> set[str]:\n70\t    \"\"\"Tool names declared in the shared host↔image mise fragment.\"\"\"\n71\t    text = SHARED_FRAGMENT.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n72\t    body = text.split(\"[tools]\", 1)[1]\n73\t    return {\n74\t        match.group(1).strip('\"')\n75\t        for match in re.finditer(r'^([A-Za-z0-9_.\"\\-:]+)\\s*=', body, re.MULTILINE)\n76\t    }\n77\t\n78\t\n79\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n80\t# The build-time check must EXECUTE, not count\n81\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n82\t\n83\t\n84\tdef test_the_dockerfile_declares_architecture_execution_probes() -> None:\n85\t    \"\"\"A named, reviewable set — not an unbounded \"run everything\" sweep.\"\"\"\n86\t    match = _PROBES_RE.search(_dockerfile())\n87\t    assert match is not None, (\n88\t        'no `ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES=\"...\"` in the Dockerfile — the build has no '\n89\t        \"check that a mise-installed binary can actually run on the target \"\n90\t        \"architecture, which is how #698 survived to smoke\"\n91\t    )\n92\t    assert len(match.group(\"names\").split()) >= 3\n93\t\n94\t\n95\tdef test_every_execution_probe_is_a_tool_the_image_installs() -> None:\n96\t    \"\"\"A probe naming an uninstalled tool can only fail — or become a no-op.\n97\t\n98\t    Cross-checked against the shared fragment rather than against a second list\n99\t    here, so a tool rename breaks this test instead of the image build.\n100\t    \"\"\"\n101\t    match = _PROBES_RE.search(_dockerfile())\n102\t    assert match is not None\n103\t    declared = _declared_tools()\n104\t\n105\t    unknown = [name for name in match.group(\"names\").split() if name not in declared]\n106\t\n107\t    assert unknown == [], (\n108\t        f\"execution probes name tools the shared fragment does not declare: \"\n109\t        f\"{unknown}. Declared: {sorted(declared)}\"\n110\t    )\n111\t\n112\t\n113\tdef test_the_probe_loop_runs_the_binary_rather_than_testing_for_it() -> None:\n114\t    \"\"\"#698's whole lesson: presence is not capability.\n115\t\n116\t    A wrong-architecture binary is present, is executable by its mode bits, and\n117\t    is counted by `mise ls --installed`. The only thing it cannot do is run.\n118\t    \"\"\"\n119\t    text = _dockerfile()\n120\t    probe_block = text.split(\"ARCH_EXEC_PROBES\", 1)[1]\n121\t\n122\t    assert \"--version\" in probe_block.split(\"\\nRUN \", 2)[1], (\n123\t        \"the architecture probe block never invokes a probe binary — a check \"\n124\t        \"that only stats the file reproduces the defect it exists to catch\"\n125\t    )\n126\t\n127\t\n128\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n129\t# The single-architecture downloads must be gated — and the gate must be armed\n130\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n131\t\n132\t\n133\tdef test_each_single_architecture_download_is_gated_on_target_arch() -> None:\n134\t    \"\"\"Graphviz ships no Linux arm64 deb; gcc-latest ships none by policy.\"\"\"\n135\t    text = _dockerfile()\n136\t    for arg in _SINGLE_ARCH_DOWNLOADS:\n137\t        run_block = next(\n138\t            block\n139\t            for block in text.split(\"\\nRUN \")[1:]\n140\t            if f\"${{{arg}}}\" in block and \"curl\" in block\n141\t        )\n142\t        assert \"TARGETARCH\" in run_block, (\n143\t            f\"the {arg} download is not gated on TARGETARCH — it is an amd64-only \"\n144\t            f\"artifact, so an arm64 build fails on it (measured: graphviz \"\n145\t            f\"`exit code: 100`, job 93363618928)\"\n146\t        )\n147\t\n148\t\n149\tdef test_every_stage_that_reads_targetarch_also_declares_it() -> None:\n150\t    \"\"\"The trap that would make this whole change a silent no-op.\n151\t\n152\t    ``TARGETARCH`` is an automatic build arg: BuildKit provides its VALUE, but\n153\t    only to stages that declare `ARG TARGETARCH`. Undeclared, it expands to the\n154\t    empty string — so `[ \"${TARGETARCH}\" = \"amd64\" ]` is false on amd64 too, and\n155\t    the amd64 image silently loses graphviz and GCC-17 while the build stays\n156\t    green. Nothing else in this repo would notice until smoke.\n157\t    \"\"\"\n158\t    declares = re.compile(r\"^ARG\\s+TARGETARCH\", re.MULTILINE)\n159\t    offenders = [\n160\t        name\n161\t        for name, body in _stages(_dockerfile()).items()\n162\t        if \"${TARGETARCH}\" in body and not declares.search(body)\n163\t    ]\n164\t\n165\t    assert offenders == [], (\n166\t        f\"stages read ${{TARGETARCH}} without declaring `ARG TARGETARCH`: \"\n167\t        f\"{offenders}. An undeclared automatic arg is EMPTY, so every \"\n168\t        f\"architecture takes the else-branch\"\n169\t    )\n170\t\n171\t\n172\tdef test_targetarch_is_only_compared_against_docker_architecture_names() -> None:\n173\t    \"\"\"``TARGETARCH`` is ``amd64``/``arm64`` — never ``x86_64``/``aarch64``.\n174\t\n175\t    Three spellings of one axis meet in this file: `uname -m` says x86_64,\n176\t    docker says amd64, mise says linux-x64. A comparison against the wrong\n177\t    spelling is always false, so the guarded branch never runs and the build\n178\t    stays green while silently doing the other thing — the same shape as the\n179\t    undeclared-ARG trap, reached by a different mistake.\n180\t    \"\"\"\n181\t    compared = set(re.findall(r'\\$\\{TARGETARCH\\}\"?\\s*!?=\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"', _dockerfile()))\n182\t\n183\t    assert compared, \"no ${TARGETARCH} comparison found — the gating is gone\"\n184\t    assert compared <= set(platform_target.PUBLISHED_ARCHES), (\n185\t        f\"TARGETARCH compared against non-docker architecture name(s): \"\n186\t        f\"{sorted(compared - set(platform_target.PUBLISHED_ARCHES))}. \"\n187\t        f\"BuildKit sets TARGETARCH to {list(platform_target.PUBLISHED_ARCHES)}\"\n188\t    )\n189\t\n190\t\n191\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n192\t# The smoke must check what the image actually contains\n193\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n194\t\n195\t\n196\tdef test_the_dockerfile_gate_and_the_smoke_agree_on_who_ships_gcc_latest() -> None:\n197\t    \"\"\"One fact stated in two languages must be bound, not trusted.\n198\t\n199\t    The Dockerfile decides with a shell comparison on `TARGETARCH`; the smoke\n200\t    script decides from `GCC_LATEST_ARCHES`. Left unbound, they drift into the\n201\t    two failure modes that look nothing alike: an image carrying a compiler\n202\t    nothing verifies, or a smoke demanding one that was never installed.\n203\t    \"\"\"\n204\t    gcc_run = next(\n205\t        block\n206\t        for block in _dockerfile().split(\"\\nRUN \")[1:]\n207\t        if \"${GCC_LATEST_DEB}\" in block and \"curl\" in block\n208\t    )\n209\t    gated_on = set(re.findall(r'\\$\\{TARGETARCH\\}\"?\\s*!?=\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"', gcc_run))\n210\t\n211\t    assert gated_on == set(platform_target.GCC_LATEST_ARCHES)\n212\t\n213\t\n214\tdef test_the_smoke_skips_gcc_latest_where_the_image_omits_it() -> None:\n215\t    \"\"\"An arm64 smoke must not fail on a compiler upstream never published.\"\"\"\n216\t    script = image.build_tier3_script(\n217\t        expected_p2996_ref=\"a\" * 40, emulated=False, gcc_latest=False\n218\t    )\n219\t\n220\t    assert \"GCC_LATEST_PRESENT=''\\n\" in script\n221\t\n222\t\n223\tdef test_the_smoke_still_demands_gcc_latest_where_the_image_ships_it() -> None:\n224\t    \"\"\"Control arm: the skip above is only safe if the check is real elsewhere.\n225\t\n226\t    A gate that skips on every architecture is indistinguishable from a deleted\n227\t    gate, and this is exactly the arm that would not have been run.\n228\t    \"\"\"\n229\t    script = image.build_tier3_script(\n230\t        expected_p2996_ref=\"a\" * 40, emulated=False, gcc_latest=True\n231\t    )\n232\t\n233\t    assert \"GCC_LATEST_PRESENT=1\\n\" in script\n234\t    assert \"/opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++\" in script\n235\t\n236\t\n237\tdef test_the_smoke_derives_gcc_latest_from_the_platform_it_is_given() -> None:\n238\t    \"\"\"The caller states an architecture, not a boolean it had to work out.\n239\t\n240\t    `build_smoke_docker_cmd` already resolves `emulated` this way; deciding\n241\t    `gcc_latest` anywhere else would put the asymmetry in a second place.\n242\t    \"\"\"\n243\t    amd64 = image.build_smoke_docker_cmd(\"img\", platform=\"linux/amd64/v2\")\n244\t    arm64 = image.build_smoke_docker_cmd(\"img\", platform=\"linux/arm64/v8\")\n245\t\n246\t    assert \"GCC_LATEST_PRESENT=1\\n\" in amd64[-1]\n247\t    assert \"GCC_LATEST_PRESENT=''\\n\" in arm64[-1]\n248\t\n249\t\n250\tdef test_the_stage_split_really_finds_the_image_stages() -> None:\n251\t    \"\"\"Control arm: the check above is vacuous if no stage body is parsed.\n252\t\n253\t    Every assertion here is a \"no offenders found\" shape, which an empty parse\n254\t    satisfies for free.\n255\t    \"\"\"\n256\t    stages = _stages(_dockerfile())\n257\t\n258\t    assert \"devcontainer\" in stages\n259\t    assert any(\"${TARGETARCH}\" in body for body in stages.values()), (\n260\t        \"no stage body mentions ${TARGETARCH} — either the gating is gone or \"\n261\t        \"the parser stopped seeing stage bodies\"\n262\t    )\n263\t"}]},"uuid":"4d6a784c-621e-4841-acc3-a97756ef84c1","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:26:58.269Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6c7c52b9-d847-40b4-afc6-731c7ab71069","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"b2793e43-9efd-442a-b982-8717c8d8c3ca","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdtbMrj61LaZy43RUvDEU","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Tb21tFW1Me7hJtYgdT9Jya","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport re,collections\nfor f in [\".devcontainer/mise-system.lock\", \".devcontainer/mise-runtime.lock\"]:\n    text=open(f).read()\n    tools = re.findall(r'^\\[tools\\.(?:\"([^\"]+)\"|([^\\]\\.]+))\\]\\s*$', text, re.M)\n    names=[a or b for a,b in tools]\n    # count platform subtables per tool\n    blocks = re.split(r'^\\[tools\\.', text, flags=re.M)\n    plat=collections.Counter()\n    per={}\n    for b in blocks[1:]:\n        name=b.split(']')[0]\n        if '.' in name and not name.startswith('\"'):\n            continue\n        ps=set(re.findall(r'^\\[tools\\.[^\\]]*\\.platforms\\.([a-z0-9\\-]+)\\]', '[tools.'+b, re.M))\n        per.setdefault(name,set()).update(ps)\n    print(f, \"tools:\", len(per))\n    c=collections.Counter()\n    for n,ps in per.items():\n        c[frozenset(ps)]+=1\n    for k,v in c.most_common():\n        print(\"  \", sorted(k), v)\nEOF","description":"Analyze lock platform coverage per tool"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":821,"cache_read_input_tokens":184306,"output_tokens":950,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":821},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":950,"cache_read_input_tokens":184306,"cache_creation_input_tokens":821,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":821,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdtbMjmdTcHaxzkhcMdWa","attributionSkill":"code-review","type":"assistant","uuid":"2dbff5fc-ab43-41f2-8179-786777d089d3","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:27:35.459Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"6c674ce5-dabc-44a2-a7e6-6721dd57e519","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Pa8uMtoVmXmyv7UoRGrYdW","type":"tool_result","content":"commit a126a64d6af51eb03f28324a067d4465879a88d2\nAuthor: Raymond Manaloto <ray.manaloto@gmail.com>\nDate:   Mon Aug 10 01:22:14 2026 -0500\n\n    fix(image): build each architecture with its own tools and packages (#698)\n    \n    #676 published an arm64 image; its CONTENTS were still amd64. Three\n    independent causes, each of which survived the build because every\n    build-time self-check counted or stat'd rather than executed.\n    \n    1. `mise-system.toml` pinned `arch = \"x86_64\"`, so any image resolved\n       x86_64 tools regardless of its build target. Deleted: each matrix leg\n       builds inside a container of its own architecture, so mise's native\n       detection already IS the target and a literal can only disagree with\n       it. `lockfile_platforms` now covers every published architecture, and\n       both image locks were regenerated in-container — system 0 -> 132\n       linux-arm64 entries, runtime 0 -> 35, tool counts unchanged (49/22)\n       and macos-x64 coverage preserved.\n    \n    2. Two hand-downloaded single-architecture `.deb`s. Probed upstream\n       before implementing, per the ticket's first AC: graphviz publishes no\n       Linux arm64 deb (44 files in the 15.1.0 release; its only arm64\n       artefact is a macOS .pkg, which is also the control arm proving the\n       negative is real), and gcc-latest publishes none BY POLICY — the\n       maintainer states \"only for x86_64\" on the index page. So graphviz\n       falls back to apt on other architectures, and arm64 ships without\n       gcc-latest. Ray's call, 2026-08-10, recorded as D31 in the spec: the\n       image is deliberately NOT architecture-symmetric, arm64's modern-GCC\n       slot goes to conda-forge gcc (linux-aarch64: 204 files at 16.1.0,\n       identical coverage to linux-64), and \"exactly 3 gcc compilers\"\n       becomes an arch-dependent invariant.\n    \n    3. Nothing executed a mise-installed binary. A wrong-architecture tool\n       downloads, installs, lists and stats exactly like a correct one; the\n       only thing it cannot do is run. `ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` runs six of\n       them, spanning backends because the backend is what resolves the\n       platform. {"parentUuid":"fd9a556b-9831-43ed-9d29-acf4e56c2e77","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KNv32z32cX7vjupaiycWJC","type":"tool_result","content":"    platform_arch,\n    resolve_platform,\n)\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    from collections.abc import Callable, Sequence\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n#: The committed lock whose platform coverage defines what a regen must produce.\nSYSTEM_LOCK = \".devcontainer/mise-system.lock\"\n\n#: Where the pinned mise binary is installed inside the stage directory.\nPINNED_MISE_DIRNAME = \"mise-pinned\"\n\nMISE_INSTALLER_URL = \"https://mise.run\"\n\n#: `mise lock` resolves through GitHub, and anonymous quota exhausts mid-run;\n#: each pass fills what the previous could not. CI uses five and converges.\nDEFAULT_PASSES = 5\n\n#: The only host that can write a faithful image lock (gotcha 1).\nREQUIRED_OS = \"Linux\"\n\n\ndef required_machines(target_platform: str | None = None) -> frozenset[str]:\n    \"\"\"Every ``uname -m`` spelling a host may report and still match the image.\n\n    Derived from the one platform parameter (#673) rather than pinned to\n    amd64: the lock has to be written on a host of the IMAGE's architecture,\n    so when #676 publishes arm64 this gate follows the target instead of\n    silently continuing to demand x86_64. Both spellings are accepted because\n    ``uname -m`` and docker disagree on the name.\n    \"\"\"\n    arch = platform_arch(resolve_platform(target_platform))\n    return frozenset({expected_uname_machine(f\"linux/{arch}\"), arch})\n\n\nclass ImageLockError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"A precondition failed, or a step of the recipe did.\"\"\"\n\n\ndef lock_platforms(lock_text: str) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Every platform the committed lock carries, tool entries and conda alike.\n\n    Derived rather than declared (gotcha 2). A hard-coded ``linux-x64`` is\n    correct only while no tool is bumped, which is the one condition under\n    which anyone runs this.\n    \"\"\"\n    covered: set[str] = set(conda_platforms(lock_text))\n    for platforms in tool_platforms(lock_text).values():\n        covered |= platforms\n    return tuple(sorted(covered))\n\n\ndef platforms_to_lock(\n    lock_text: str, *, required: Sequence[str] | None = None\n) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n    \"\"\"What a regen must write: what the lock HAS, plus what it OWES.\n\n    :func:`lock_platforms` alone can only ever *preserve* coverage — it reads\n    the committed file, so an architecture that was just added to the publish\n    matrix has no entries yet and is therefore skipped by the very run meant to\n    create them. The regen then reports success across the platforms it already\n    had, and #698's symptom (an arm64 image resolving x86_64 tools) survives its\n    own fix.\n\n    ``required`` defaults to every architecture\n    :data:`~dotfiles_setup.platform_target.PUBLISHED_ARCHES` ships, so widening\n    the publish matrix is what widens the next regen — one declaration, not two.\n    \"\"\"\n    owed = mise_lock_platforms() if required is None else tuple(required)\n    return tuple(sorted(set(lock_platforms(lock_text)) | set(owed)))\n\n\ndef host_can_lock(\n    asserted rather than assumed: the bytes are piped to ``sh``, so an\n    ``http://`` or ``file://`` override would be a code-execution primitive\n    rather than a convenience.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not url.startswith(\"https://\"):\n        msg = f\"refusing to fetch the mise installer over a non-https URL: {url}\"\n        raise ImageLockError(msg)\n    result = subprocess.run([\"curl\", \"-fsSL\", url], capture_output=True, check=False)\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        msg = (\n            f\"fetching {url} failed (rc={result.returncode}): \"\n            f\"{result.stderr.decode(errors='replace').strip()}\"\n        )\n        raise ImageLockError(msg)\n    return result.stdout\n\n\ndef install_pinned_mise(\n    stage_dir: Path,\n    version: str,\n    *,\n    fetch: Callable[[str], bytes] = fetch_installer,\n    url: str = MISE_INSTALLER_URL,\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Install the image's exact mise version into ``stage_dir``.\n\n    The pin is not optional: lock formats are **not** cross-version compatible,\n    so a lock written by a different mise is one the image's\n    ``mise install --locked`` rejects. Same installer and same ``ARG\n    MISE_VERSION`` the Dockerfile uses.\n    \"\"\"\n    install_path = stage_dir / PINNED_MISE_DIRNAME\n    env = {\n        **os.environ,\n        \"MISE_VERSION\": f\"v{version}\",\n        \"MISE_INSTALL_PATH\": str(install_path),\n    }\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"sh\"], input=fetch(url), env=env, capture_output=True, check=False\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        msg = (\n            f\"installing pinned mise v{version} failed (rc={result.returncode}): \"\n            f\"{result.stderr.decode(errors='replace').strip()}\"\n        )\n        raise ImageLockError(msg)\n    if not install_path.exists():\n        msg = f\"pinned mise installer reported success but {install_path} is absent\"\n        raise ImageLockError(msg)\n    return install_path\n\n\ndef lock_command(\n    mise_bin: Path, stage_dir: Path, platforms: tuple[str, ...]\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"The ``mise lock`` argv for one convergence pass.\"\"\"\n    argv = [str(mise_bin), \"lock\"]\n    for name in platforms:\n        argv += [\"--platform\", name]\n    return [*argv, \"-C\", str(stage_dir)]\n\n\ndef run_lock_passes(\n    mise_bin: Path,\n    stage_dir: Path,\n    platforms: tuple[str, ...],\n    *,\n    passes: int = DEFAULT_PASSES,\n    run: Callable[..., subprocess.CompletedProcess[bytes]] = subprocess.run,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Run the convergence loop; the LAST pass must succeed.\n\n    Earlier passes are allowed to fail — that is what the loop is for, since\n    exhausted GitHub quota is the expected mid-run failure. A final failure is\n    real: ``mise lock`` has hard-errored on an unresolvable tool since\n    2026.6.13, so a genuinely broken tool fails loud here rather than producing\n    a quietly short lock.\n    \"\"\"\n    argv = lock_command(mise_bin, stage_dir, platforms)\n    child_env = {\n        **os.environ,\n        # Both tiers in one pass: mise writes mise.lock (base) and\n        # mise.runtime.lock (runtime tier) under this env (#160 T9).\n        \"MISE_ENV\": RUNTIME_ENV,\n        \"MISE_TRUSTED_CONFIG_PATHS\": str(stage_dir),\n    }\n    last: subprocess.CompletedProcess[bytes] | None = None\n    for attempt in range(1, passes + 1):\n        last = run(argv, env=child_env, check=False)\n        if last.returncode == 0:\n            logger.info(\"mise lock converged on pass %d/%d\", attempt, passes)\n            return\n        logger.warning(\n            \"mise lock pass %d/%d exited %d — retrying (rate limits are the \"\n            \"expected cause)\",\n            attempt,\n            passes,\n            last.returncode,\n        )\n    rc = last.returncode if last is not None else -1\n    msg = f\"mise lock did not converge in {passes} pass(es); last rc={rc}\"\n    raise ImageLockError(msg)\n\n\ndef container_command(repo_root: Path, extra: tuple[str, ...] = ()) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Re-invoke this task inside the amd64 devcontainer.\n\n    ``devcontainer exec`` and not raw ``docker exec`` (``do-not.md`` #3), and\n    the inner call passes ``--no-container`` so the recursion terminates. The\n    workspace is bind-mounted, so the inner run's writes land on the host tree.\n    \"\"\"\n    return [\n        \"devcontainer\",\n        \"exec\",\n        \"--workspace-folder\",\n        str(repo_root),\n        \"mise\",\n        \"exec\",\n        \"--\",\n        \"uv\",\n        \"run\",","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"86158250-3019-4d53-8416-29f736edd51e","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:28:14.572Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fd9a556b-9831-43ed-9d29-acf4e56c2e77","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"ef0908e3-aa68-4547-a630-eaf824b008cd","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BtfkVJAAE2V1CEpNPvcxcg","type":"tool_result","content":"python/verification/suites.toml:327:name = \"build.gcc-latest-deb-checksum\"\npython/verification/suites.toml:328:description = \"Validation D (#160 T13): the gcc-latest .deb must be sha256-verified in the same RUN that downloads it — kayari publishes no checksum/signature, so the committed GCC_LATEST_DEB_SHA256 ARG is the only integrity handle for those bytes\"\npython/verification/suites.toml:339:name = \"build.gcc-latest-reflection\"\npython/verification/suites.toml:340:description = \"GCC-17 with P2996 reflection must be installed via precompiled .deb — and, since #698, ONLY on the architectures that have one. kayari.org publishes the gcc-latest trunk snapshot for x86_64 alone and says so as policy on its index page ('only for x86_64'), so an ungated download fails the arm64 base build outright. Both the install and its reflection smoke therefore carry a TARGETARCH branch, and each branch's skip message is bound here: deleting a branch is the realistic regression, and it would otherwise surface only as a 30-minute base build failing on a .deb that cannot exist. The `-freflection` token pins the smoke's own compile line (1x in the file; bare `-freflection` matches 5x and `/tmp/refl-gcc.cpp` 3x, so neither alone binds a site).\"\npython/verification/suites.toml:346:    \"gcc-latest.deb\",\npython/verification/suites.toml:347:    \"test -x /opt/gcc-latest/bin/g++\",\npython/verification/suites.toml:348:    \"-freflection \\\\\\n        /tmp/refl-gcc.cpp\",\npython/verification/suites.toml:349:    \"gcc-latest: SKIPPED on \",\npython/verification/suites.toml:350:    \"gcc-latest reflection smoke: SKIPPED on \",\npython/verification/suites.toml:355:description = \"#698: publishing an architecture is not the same as BUILDING one. #676 shipped an arm64 image whose mise config pinned `arch = \\\"x86_64\\\"`, so 131 tool entries resolved for the wrong CPU — and the build never noticed, because every build-time self-check COUNTS or STATS (`mise ls --installed | wc -l`, `test -d`, a non-empty shims dir) and a wrong-architecture binary is present, executable by its mode bits, and counted exactly like a correct one. The only thing it cannot do is run, so `ARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` + its loop RUN it; the failure message is bound rather than the loop body because deleting the invocation and keeping a `test -x` is the regression that reproduces the original defect. Three declarations must stay bound together: `PUBLISHED_ARCHES` (what CI builds), `lockfile_platforms` (what the image resolves), and mise's own third spelling of the axis — hence `mise_lock_platforms`, and `find_lock_platform_drift`/`find_pinned_image_arch` wired into the `platform-literals` CLI that `no_platform_literals` already runs. `platforms_to_lock` is bound at its CALL SITE because the library function existing is not the point: `lock_platforms` alone derives from the COMMITTED lock, so a newly-published architecture has no entries and is skipped by the very regen meant to create them — the union with what the publish matrix owes is what breaks that circle. `ships_gcc_latest` is bound at the smoke's call site for the same reason: kayari publishes gcc-latest for x86_64 only (upstream policy, not a gap), so the image is deliberately NOT architecture-symmetric and a smoke that assumed symmetry would fail arm64 over an artifact that does not exist.\"\npython/verification/suites.toml:380:    \"def ships_gcc_latest(\",\npython/verification/suites.toml:386:    \"gcc_latest=ships_gcc_latest(platform)\",\npython/verification/suites.toml:1738: a/docs/specs/devcontainer-gcc162-dual-arch.md b/docs/specs/devcontainer-gcc162-dual-arch.md\nindex 7a0ffe4..b7a9ca0 100644\n--- a/docs/specs/devcontainer-gcc162-dual-arch.md\n+++ b/docs/specs/devcontainer-gcc162-dual-arch.md\n@@ -1582,6 +1582,53 @@ identical on a Struct. **22 lines replace 60: a net reduction.**\n \n ---\n \n+### D31 — arm64 ships WITHOUT `gcc-latest`; the image is deliberately asymmetric ✅\n+\n+**Ray's call, 2026-08-10, on evidence gathered before any implementation** —\n+#698's first acceptance criterion was a research gate precisely because a\n+negative answer would have mooted the ticket.\n+\n+**What was probed, and what the control arms were:**\n+\n+| Artefact | arm64 build? | Evidence | Control arm |\n+|---|---|---|---|\n+| graphviz `15.1.0` deb | **No** | GitLab pkg `62359347`, 44 files: four Linux debs (`ubuntu_22.04`…`26.04`), all amd64 | the same listing DOES surface `Darwin_23.6.0_…-arm64.pkg`, so the Linux-arm64 zero is a real negative |\n+| `gcc-latest` deb | **No, by policy** | jwakely.github.io states *\"Only the C and C++ compilers are included, and only for x86_64\"*; 1 deb; page arch tokens `x86_64:1 / arm64:0` | primary source is the maintainer's own prose, not an inferred absence |\n+| conda-forge `gcc` | **Yes** | `linux-aarch64`: 204 files, max 16.1.0 | `linux-64`: 204 files, max 16.1.0 — identical, so coverage is not partial |\n+\n+**The asymmetry that decided it.** `clang-p2996` is **built from source**\n+(`Dockerfile` clang-builder stage), so it is architecture-portable — arm64 needs\n+a compile, not a new artefact. GCC-17 is a **prebuilt x86_64-only `.deb`**, and\n+there is no arm64 path short of building GCC trunk from source. The named\n+upstream alternative (Fedora Rawhide's GCC-trunk COPR, *\"built for more\n+architectures\"*) ships **RPMs for Rawhide** and cannot serve an Ubuntu 26.04\n+image.\n+\n+**Consequence for R1's \"verify there are only 3 gcc compilers\":** that invariant\n+is now **architecture-dependent** — 3 on amd64, 2 on arm64 until the conda GCC\n+lands. It cannot be asserted as a constant, and `platform_target.GCC_LATEST_ARCHES`\n+is the one place the asymmetry is stated. graphviz falls back to apt's package\n+on arm64, which was the lean floor the pinned deb was chosen to beat anyway.\n+\n+⭐ **Ray's earlier \"publish both and let CI establish the real failure\" call\n+(D1) is what produced this.** The predicted failure was mise lock resolution at\n+smoke; the actual failure was `base-prep` dying ~30 minutes in on a hand-\n+downloaded amd64 `.deb`, a defect class the analysis had not considered. Neither\n+`.deb` is visible to any dependency tool here — Renovate tracks their *versions*,\n+and nothing tracked their *architectures*, because that dimension did not exist\n+while the project was single-arch.\n+\n+**Also fixed under #698, because a count cannot see this class of defect:** every\n+build-time self-check in the image *counted* or *stat*ed (`mise ls --installed |\n+wc -l`, `test -d`, a non-empty shims dir), and a wrong-architecture binary is\n+present, executable by its mode bits, and counted exactly like a correct one. The\n+new `ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` loop **runs** six mise-installed binaries. Per\n+[[probes-need-a-control-arm]] rule 9 it asserts the capability rather than\n+sniffing a symptom — no `uname -m` comparison, no ELF-header read, no version\n+string, each of which would bind something upstream owns and go quietly dead.\n+\n+---\n+\n ## ✅ Promoted to `docs/specs/` — tracked, survives a clone\n \n Done 2026-08-08 on Ray's instruction (Q17). {"parentUuid":"448e531a-a8f0-45c4-8d44-9b5dbf24c4e6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FuTQHD4HYYSVksonU8ibgq","type":"tool_result","content":"missing_tools = \"always\"\nshow_env = true\n\n[env]\n# Expose the apt LLVM-22 toolchain (#222 PR-C) on PATH via mise's native path\n# directive — NOT /etc/profile.d. mise manages PATH through activation (this is\n# how every base tool is exposed), and the chezmoi-rendered ~/.profile runs\n# `mise activate`, which resets PATH and would discard a profile.d addition. The\n# old conda clang++ survived only because it was a mise shim in a dir mise's PATH\n# includes; `_.path` restores that exact exposure for the apt binaries. mise puts\n# this FIRST in the activated PATH, so the unversioned clang/clang++/clangd/\n# clang-tidy/clang-format/lld/lldb/llvm-* win over /opt/clang-p2996 (the general\n# toolchain, matching the pre-#222 behavior). {"parentUuid":"43be6878-bdf1-4896-bee4-94dc446de102","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01R5M8mXcaq5HcFoJHK4pTNp","type":"tool_result","content":"    llvmcheck_out=\"$(/tmp/llvmcheck)\" && \\\n    [ \"$llvmcheck_out\" = \"ok\" ] && \\\n    test -x /usr/lib/llvm-22/bin/clangd && \\\n    test -x /usr/lib/llvm-22/bin/clang-tidy && \\\n    test -x /usr/lib/llvm-22/bin/clang-format && \\\n    test -x /usr/lib/llvm-22/bin/lld && \\\n    test -x /usr/lib/llvm-22/bin/lldb && \\\n    llvm_config_version=\"$(/usr/lib/llvm-22/bin/llvm-config --version)\" && \\\n    grep -q '^22' <<<\"$llvm_config_version\" && \\\n    rm -f /tmp/llvmcheck.cpp /tmp/llvmcheck\n\n# Pre-install tools globally via mise\n# Known cosmetic warnings during install:\n# - rustup \"existing settings file\": rustup-init creates settings.toml then\n#   check_existence_of_settings_file() warns about its own file. Fixed via\n#   RUSTUP_INIT_SKIP_EXISTENCE_CHECKS=yes (undocumented env var, see\n#   rust-lang/rustup/src/cli/self_update.rs). RUSTUP_INIT_SKIP_PATH_CHECK\n#   in mise-system.toml [env] is a different check (PATH validation).\n# - bun \"add the global bin folder to $PATH\": bun checks if its bin output\n#   directory exists on $PATH at install time. During Docker RUN, PATH only\n#   includes /usr/local/share/mise/shims — not bun's per-package bin dirs. At runtime,\n#   mise shims intercept all lookups so the warning is cosmetic. No bun env\n#   var (BUN_QUIET, BUN_LOG_LEVEL) suppresses this — it's hardcoded in bun's\n#   install reporter. Unfixable: fundamental mismatch between bun's PATH-based\n#   assumptions and mise's shim resolution model.\nRUN --mount=type=cache,id=mise-cache,target=/usr/local/share/mise/cache,sharing=locked \\\n    --mount=type=cache,id=uv-cache,target=/root/.cache/uv,sharing=locked \\\n    --mount=type=secret,id=github_token \\\n    export MISE_INSTALL_MODE=system && \\\n    export UV_LINK_MODE=copy && \\\n    export LANG=C.UTF-8 && \\\n    export LC_ALL=C.UTF-8 && \\\n    export RUSTUP_INIT_SKIP_EXISTENCE_CHECKS=yes && \\\n    if [ -f /run/secrets/github_token ]; then \\\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=\"$(cat /run/secrets/github_token)\" && \\\n      export GITHUB_TOKEN && \\\n      export MISE_GITHUB_TOKEN=\"$GITHUB_TOKEN\"; \\\n    fi && \\\n    mise install --system --locked -y && \\\n    test \"$(mise ls --installed | wc -l)\" -gt 0 || { echo \"ERROR: mise installed zero tools — system config not discovered (check MISE_CONFIG_DIR points at /usr/local/share/mise containing config.toml)\"; exit 1; } && \\\n    test -d /usr/local/share/cargo/bin || { echo \"ERROR: MISE_CARGO_HOME not honored — /usr/local/share/cargo/bin missing after mise install (rust cookbook regression)\"; exit 1; } && \\\n    test -d /usr/local/share/rustup/toolchains || { echo \"ERROR: MISE_RUSTUP_HOME not honored — /usr/local/share/rustup/toolchains missing after mise install (rust cookbook regression)\"; exit 1; } && \\\n    chmod -R 777 /usr/local/share/mise /usr/local/share/cargo /usr/local/share/rustup\n\n# Reshim to ensure all shims are present (aqua tools may include doc-file\n# shims like LICENSE, README — these are harmless and must not be deleted\n# or mise doctor will report them as missing)\nRUN mise reshim -f && \\\n    test -n \"$(find /usr/local/share/mise/shims -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -print -quit)\" || { echo \"ERROR: /usr/local/share/mise/shims empty after reshim — mise has no installed tools to shim\"; exit 1; }\n\n# Architecture EXECUTION check (#698). Every self-check above counts or stats:\n# `mise ls --installed | wc -l`, `test -d`, a non-empty shims dir. None of them\n# can see the defect this exists for — a tool resolved for the wrong platform\n# downloads, installs, lists and stats exactly like a correct one. The only\n# thing it cannot do is RUN, so this runs it.\n#\n# Assert the capability, never a symptom ([[probes-need-a-control-arm]] rule 9):\n# no `uname -m` comparison, no ELF-header sniffing, no version string — each of\n# those binds something we do not own and goes quietly dead when it changes.\n# A binary that executes on this CPU is the whole claim.\n#\n# The names are cross-checked against `.config/mise/conf.d/shared.toml` by\n# `test_every_execution_probe_is_a_tool_the_image_installs`, so a tool rename\n# fails a unit test rather than a 30-minute base build. They deliberately span\n# backends (aqua/ubi/github/cargo) — the backend is what resolves the platform,\n# so one probe per download path is what makes this more than a spot check.\nARG ARCH_EXEC_PROBES=\"jq shfmt hk pkl uv chezmoi\"\nRUN checked=0; \\\n    for probe in ${ARCH_EXEC_PROBES}; do \\\n      bin=\"/usr/local/share/mise/shims/${probe}\"; \\\n      test -x \"$bin\" || { echo \"ERROR: expected mise shim ${probe} is missing\"; exit 1; }; \\\n      \"$bin\" --version >/dev/null || { echo \"ERROR: ${probe} INSTALLED but did not execute on $(uname -m) — its tool was resolved for another architecture (#698)\"; exit 1; }; \\\n      checked=$((checked + 1)); \\\n    done; \\\n    test \"$checked\" -ge 3 || { echo \"ERROR: architecture execution check ran only ${checked} probe(s) — ARCH_EXEC_PROBES is empty or was not expanded\"; exit 1; }; \\\n    echo \"OK: ${checked} mise-installed tools execute natively on $(uname -m)\"\n\n# Final global permissions check\nRUN chmod -R 777 /usr/local/share/mise /usr/local/share/cargo /usr/local/share/rustup\n\n# Copy image-specific hk config + shared checks for smoke-test validation.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"d37756ea-4083-4052-b9d0-43f4416af484","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:29:49.675Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"43be6878-bdf1-4896-bee4-94dc446de102","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

The Python\n        # check fails on `missing`/`version_mismatch` AND on an unexpectedly\n        # unavailable apt manager or set drift — conditions the build-time\n        # `status --missing` exit code cannot see (unavailable managers emit\n        # state \"skipped\"). Plain `status --json` exits 0 even with gaps, so\n        # the JSON is always captured for the artifact; the assert gates.\n        if: steps.probe.outputs.hit != 'true'\n        env:\n          IMAGE_REF: ${{ env.CONTAINER_REGISTRY }}/${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}:${{ steps.smoke-tag.outputs.tag }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          docker run --rm \"${IMAGE_REF}\" \\\n            mise bootstrap packages status --json > /tmp/bootstrap-gap-report.json\n          uv run --project python dotfiles-setup bootstrap-gap-report \\\n            --status-json /tmp/bootstrap-gap-report.json\n      - name: Upload bootstrap gap report artifact\n        if: always()\n        uses: actions/upload-artifact@043fb46d1a93c77aae656e7c1c64a875d1fc6a0a # v7.0.1\n        with:\n          name: bootstrap-gap-report-${{ matrix.target.arch }}\n          path: /tmp/bootstrap-gap-report.json\n          if-no-files-found: ignore\n        # smoke (+ the bootstrap gap report above) is the PR-blocking image\n        # gate. {"parentUuid":"72849c45-e85c-4bdb-9ff7-8e1577c2821a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdtbcqtuFrddz9BSAHtN4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01AsDiWUnCjDcoQrSUMhbW2y","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"sed -n '955,1045p' .github/workflows/build-publish.yml","description":"Read dev-tag job"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1428,"cache_read_input_tokens":219876,"output_tokens":3543,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1428},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":3543,"cache_read_input_tokens":219876,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1428,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1428,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdtbcoZWC3fJ7aY6wP6xH","attributionSkill":"code-review","type":"assistant","uuid":"ebb19f3d-87a3-4707-bc1f-09ea1bbdfd4b","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:31:34.819Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}
{"parentUuid":"ebb19f3d-87a3-4707-bc1f-09ea1bbdfd4b","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AsDiWUnCjDcoQrSUMhbW2y","type":"tool_result","content":"  # image that cleared smoke, so a future probe HIT reuses a\n  # validated image, never an untested one. Runs whenever build+smoke ran\n  # and passed (PR cache-miss AND nightly), so the marker stays fresh.\n  # Recomputes dev-hash independently (deterministic) since dev-prep is\n  # skipped on the nightly path.\n  # ============================================================\n  dev-tag:\n    needs: [plan, build, smoke-test]\n    if: needs.smoke-test.result == 'success'\n    strategy:\n      fail-fast: false\n      matrix:\n        target: ${{ fromJSON(needs.plan.outputs.matrix) }}\n    # This job only moves tags, so it does not need a native runner — but it\n    # DOES need `dev-hash` to resolve this leg's architecture, and that reads\n    # PLATFORM. Running on the matching runner keeps the two facts in one place\n    # rather than making the label and the exported platform independently\n    # correct-by-inspection.\n    runs-on: ${{ matrix.target.runner }}\n    timeout-minutes: 15\n    permissions:\n      contents: read\n      packages: write\n    env:\n      PLATFORM: ${{ matrix.target.platform }}\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout code\n        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n        with:\n          ref: ${{ inputs.ref }}\n          persist-credentials: false\n      - name: Install mise (python + uv)\n        uses: ./.github/actions/setup-mise\n        with:\n          install_args: \"python uv\"\n      - name: Set up Docker Buildx\n        uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@bb05f3f5519dd87d3ba754cc423b652a5edd6d2c # v4.2.0\n      - name: Log in to Container Registry\n        uses: docker/login-action@dbcb813823bdd20940b903addbd779551569679f # v4.6.0\n        with:\n          registry: ${{ env.CONTAINER_REGISTRY }}\n          username: ${{ github.actor }}\n          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n      - name: Resolve p2996 ref override\n        # Phase D (#120): the validated marker is content-addressed by\n        # dev-hash, which folds in CLANG_P2996_REF. dev-tag MUST honor the\n        # same override the p2996 cache built with — otherwise it would\n        # stamp a pinned-ref hash onto overridden-ref content, poisoning a\n        # later canonical build's probe. See p2996-prep for rationale.\n        if: inputs.p2996_ref != ''\n        env:\n          P2996_REF: ${{ inputs.p2996_ref }}\n        run: echo \"CLANG_P2996_REF=${P2996_REF}\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\"\n      - name: Tag validated image as :dev-<hash>\n        env:\n          IMAGE: ${{ env.CONTAINER_REGISTRY }}/${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}\n          COMMIT_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}\n          TAG_SUFFIX: ${{ matrix.target.tag_suffix }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          # `dev-hash` resolves PLATFORM, so this is THIS leg's hash and the\n          # marker it stamps is per-architecture without any suffix of its own\n          # (#676). Stamping it from the per-architecture source tag is what\n          # keeps that true: marking a multi-architecture index with a\n          # single-architecture hash would make the other leg's probe hit an\n          # image it never validated.\n          hash=$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup dev-hash)\n          short_sha=$(printf '%s' \"${COMMIT_SHA}\" | head -c 7)\n          source_ref=\"${IMAGE}:${short_sha}-${TAG_SUFFIX}\"\n          marker_ref=\"${IMAGE}:dev-${hash}\"\n          # The just-built + smoked image is published at :sha-<sha>;\n          # stamp the content-hash marker onto that exact digest.\n          docker buildx imagetools create --tag \"${marker_ref}\" \"${source_ref}\"\n          source_digest=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${source_ref}\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}')\n          marker_digest=$(docker buildx imagetools inspect \"${marker_ref}\" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}')\n          [ -n \"${marker_digest}\" ] || { echo \"FAIL: empty digest after marker tag\" >&2; exit 1; }\n          if [ \"${marker_digest}\" != \"${source_digest}\" ]; then\n            echo \"FAIL: marker tag did not propagate the validated digest\" >&2\n            echo \"  source : ${source_digest}\" >&2\n            echo \"  marker : ${marker_digest}\" >&2\n            exit 1\n          fi\n          {\n            echo \"## Dev cache marker tagged\"\n            echo \"\"\n            echo \"- \\`${marker_ref}\\` → digest \\`${source_digest}\\` (smoke-validated)\"\n          } >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n  # ============================================================\n  # Manifest merge (#676): the moving tags become an INDEX.\n  #\n  # Every build leg publishes exactly one `:<sha>-<arch>` tag and nothing else,","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"8ddfa1da-414f-4e4b-8788-5dd118427728","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:31:35.737Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ebb19f3d-87a3-4707-bc1f-09ea1bbdfd4b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"bf0596ed-2aea-4125-87d4-8411f61fa398","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"5bfde11b-2bd4-4490-a0cf-7e623745dd9d","agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VEZJLmGfgWF1MSD7UbtyhK","type":"tool_result","content":"diff --git a/tests/test_platform_target.py b/tests/test_platform_target.py\nindex 75521f2..8e51b2d 100644\n--- a/tests/test_platform_target.py\n+++ b/tests/test_platform_target.py\n@@ -460,3 +460,114 @@ def test_published_arch_without_a_runner_label_raises(\n     monkeypatch.setattr(platform_target, \"PUBLISHED_ARCHES\", (\"amd64\", \"riscv64\"))\n     with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=\"riscv64\"):\n         platform_target.published_targets()\n+\n+\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n+# The image's OWN architecture (#698) — publishing an arch is not building it\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n+\n+\n+def test_every_published_arch_has_a_mise_lock_platform() -> None:\n+    \"\"\"Mise spells the same axis a third way, and the locks are keyed by it.\n+\n+    `PUBLISHED_ARCHES` says what CI builds, docker says `amd64`/`arm64`, and\n+    mise's lockfiles say `linux-x64`/`linux-arm64`. An architecture published\n+    without a lock-platform name resolves its tools for somebody else's CPU.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    platforms = platform_target.mise_lock_platforms()\n+    assert len(platforms) == len(platform_target.PUBLISHED_ARCHES)\n+    assert len(set(platforms)) == len(platforms)\n+\n+\n+def test_a_published_arch_without_a_lock_platform_raises(\n+    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n+) -> None:\n+    \"\"\"FAIL arm: an unmapped architecture must not silently resolve to nothing.\n+\n+    Declared through `PUBLISHED_ARCHES` — the edit a person actually makes.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    monkeypatch.setattr(platform_target, \"PUBLISHED_ARCHES\", (\"amd64\", \"riscv64\"))\n+    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=\"riscv64\"):\n+        platform_target.mise_lock_platforms()\n+\n+\n+def test_the_image_config_locks_every_published_architecture() -> None:\n+    \"\"\"#698: the image resolved x86_64 tools while CI published two arches.\n+\n+    `lockfile_platforms` scopes what `mise lock` writes, so an architecture\n+    missing from it gets **no lock entries at all** — and `mise install\n+    --locked` then resolves that architecture's tools from somebody else's\n+    platform. It survives the build (the self-checks counted tools rather than\n+    running them), so nothing fails until a binary is executed.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    assert platform_target.find_lock_platform_drift(REPO_ROOT) is None\n+\n+\n+def test_an_uncovered_architecture_is_reported(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n+    \"\"\"FAIL arm: the gate above is decoration unless a narrow list really fails.\"\"\"\n+    config = tmp_path / \".devcontainer\" / \"mise-system.toml\"\n+    config.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n+    config.write_text(\n+        '[settings]\\nlockfile_platforms = [\"linux-x64\"]\\n', encoding=\"utf-8\"\n+    )\n+\n+    message = platform_target.find_lock_platform_drift(tmp_path)\n+\n+    assert message is not None\n+    assert \"linux-arm64\" in message\n+\n+\n+def test_a_deleted_lockfile_platforms_declaration_is_reported(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n+    \"\"\"A missing declaration must not read as 'every architecture is covered'.\n+\n+    mise's own default is every platform it knows, so a reader could argue the\n+    absence is harmless. It is not: the declaration is what `image_lock.py`\n+    reads to decide which platforms to regenerate, so deleting it silently\n+    narrows the refresh instead of widening it.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    config = tmp_path / \".devcontainer\" / \"mise-system.toml\"\n+    config.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n+    config.write_text(\"[settings]\\nexperimental = true\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+\n+    message = platform_target.find_lock_platform_drift(tmp_path)\n+\n+    assert message is not None\n+    assert \"lockfile_platforms\" in message\n+\n+\n+def test_the_image_config_does_not_pin_a_literal_architecture() -> None:\n+    \"\"\"#698 AC1: `arch` is derived from the build target, never a literal.\n+\n+    Each matrix leg builds in a container OF its target architecture, so mise's\n+    own detection is already the target. A literal `arch` overrides that\n+    detection with whatever was true when the line was written.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    assert platform_target.find_pinned_image_arch(REPO_ROOT) is None\n+\n+\n+def test_a_literal_arch_pin_in_the_image_config_is_reported(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n+    \"\"\"FAIL arm: restore the exact line #698 was filed for.\"\"\"\n+    config = tmp_path / \".devcontainer\" / \"mise-system.toml\"\n+    config.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n+    config.write_text('[settings]\\narch = \"x86_64\"\\n', encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+\n+    message = platform_target.find_pinned_image_arch(tmp_path)\n+\n+    assert message is not None\n+    assert \"x86_64\" in message\n+\n+\n+def test_a_commented_out_arch_pin_is_not_a_violation(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n+    \"\"\"A comment explaining why the pin was removed must not re-trip the gate.\n+\n+    Without this the honest fix — deleting the line and saying why — fails the\n+    check that motivated it, and the next author deletes the explanation.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    config = tmp_path / \".devcontainer\" / \"mise-system.toml\"\n+    config.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n+    config.write_text(\n+        '[settings]\\n# arch = \"x86_64\" was removed by #698\\nexperimental = true\\n',\n+        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n+    )\n+\n+    assert platform_target.find_pinned_image_arch(tmp_path) is None","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"c4d0a303-6dba-4c9c-82f9-392e2dcb54fb","timestamp":"2026-08-10T06:32:01.152Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bf0596ed-2aea-4125-87d4-8411f61fa398","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"3735286b-9164-4e21-8650-f2bcb90c9323","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"fix/698-arm64-image-arch"}

{"parentUuid":"4fbdc66a-f097-4f40-b42e-7b18f56197f5","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a6d01340a069727e5","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdtbjrGfoRDyfq42P7EDf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"No `ReportFindings` tool is available in this session, so the findings are reported directly below.\n\n## Review scope\n\n`git diff main...HEAD` on `fix/698-arm64-image-arch` — 6 commits: the #676 dual-arch publish pipeline (already carried by open PR #700) plus the new top commit `a126a64` \"build each architecture with its own tools and packages (#698)\".\n\n## Findings\n\n**1. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:511` — HIGH. The new `:dev-<arch>` promote loop asserts a digest equality that `imagetools create` cannot satisfy, so `promote` fails on every main merge.**\nThe loop feeds a **single platform image manifest** (`${IMAGE}@${digest}`, taken from `.manifests[]` of the index) to `docker buildx imagetools create`, then requires `imagetools inspect --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}'` to return that same digest. `--prefer-index` defaults to **true**, and per Docker's CLI reference a single source that is *not* already an index is wrapped in a **new** index — so `got` is the new index's digest and never equals `digest`. Scenario: a PR merges, `:dev`/`:latest` are retagged successfully, then the very next lines exit 1 with `FAIL: :dev-amd64 is sha256:… expected sha256:…` — main CI red with `:dev` already moved. Note the sibling sites survive only because *their* sources are tags pointing at indexes (buildkit pushes an index when attestations are on): `manifest`'s `:dev-<suffix>` creation and `dev-tag`'s marker both copy an index. Fix: `--prefer-index=false`, or compare the child manifest digest inside the newly created index.\n\n**2. `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json:116` — HIGH. `MISE_ARCH=x86_64` and `CONDA_SUBDIR=linux-64` are still hard-pinned in `containerEnv`, which reproduces exactly the defect #698 removed from `[settings]`.**\nControl-armed locally: `MISE_ARCH=x86_64 mise settings get arch` → `x86_64`; unset → `mise ERROR Setting [arch] is not set`. `MISE_ARCH` *is* the environment spelling of the `arch` setting the commit deleted. `containerEnv` puts it in every process in the container, so on an arm64 devcontainer every runtime `mise install` (including the user overlay tier `on-create.sh` eagerly installs) resolves x86_64 tools, and `CONDA_SUBDIR=linux-64` forces conda/rattler to the x86_64 subdir. The new `ARCH_EXEC_PROBES` gate cannot see this — it runs at *build* time, on the system tier only.\n\n**3. `.devcontainer/mise-system.toml:364` — MEDIUM. The same `MISE_ARCH = \"x86_64\"` / `CONDA_SUBDIR = \"linux-64\"` pins survive in `[env]`, five lines below the comment explaining why the `[settings] arch` pin was deleted.**\nProbed both arms: mise's own `[env]` does **not** feed its top-level settings resolution (`[env] MISE_ARCH` → still \"not set\"), so the Dockerfile's own `mise install --system` is unaffected — but `mise exec -- mise settings get arch` under that config returns `x86_64`, i.e. any nested mise invocation or mise-activated shell inherits the pin. Combined with finding 2 the file and the JSON now disagree with the commit's own stated intent.\n\n**4. `python/src/dotfiles_setup/platform_target.py:570` — MEDIUM. `find_pinned_image_arch` can only ever see the one spelling that was already fixed.**\nIt scans a single file (`.devcontainer/mise-system.toml`) with `^\\s*arch\\s*=\\s*[\"']…`. It is structurally blind to `MISE_ARCH`, `CONDA_SUBDIR`, and to `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` entirely — the three places the pin actually still lives (findings 2 and 3). Its FAIL-arm test (`test_a_literal_arch_pin_in_the_image_config_is_reported`) restores the exact deleted line, so the gate is armed only against the regression that is least likely to recur.\n\n**5. `python/src/dotfiles_setup/image.py:1952` — MEDIUM. The tier-3 smoke-script CLI never passes `gcc_latest`, so an arm64 devcontainer's smoke demands a compiler the arm64 image deliberately omits.**\n`smoke_script_main` forces `emulated=True` but leaves `gcc_latest` at its `True` default, while `build_smoke_docker_cmd` correctly derives it via `ImageArch.for_platform`. Scenario: set `DOTFILES_PLATFORM=linux/arm64/v8` in `mise.local.toml` (the documented per-clone override) on the arm64 Mac, `mise run up`, then `mise run verify-container-latest` → tier 3 exits with `FAIL: gcc-latest binary missing` on an image that is correct by design. Derive it from `resolve_platform()` the way tier 1 derives its base-currency data.\n\n**6. `tests/test_image_arch.py:209` — LOW. The gcc-latest gating tests capture the operand but not the operator, so inverting the condition passes the whole suite.**\n`re.findall(r'\\$\\{TARGETARCH\\}\"?\\s*!?=\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"')` yields `{\"amd64\"}` for both `= \"amd64\"` and `!= \"amd64\"`. Flipping `.devcontainer/Dockerfile:565` from `!=` to `=` — installing the x86_64-only deb on arm64 and skipping it on amd64, the realistic sign-error regression — leaves `test_the_dockerfile_gate_and_the_smoke_agree_on_who_ships_gcc_latest`, `test_each_single_architecture_download_is_gated_on_target_arch` and `test_targetarch_is_only_compared_against_docker_architecture_names` all green. Capture the operator in the regex and assert the branch polarity.\n\n## Things I checked and found sound\n\n- `TARGETARCH` is declared (`ARG TARGETARCH`) in both stages that read it (`devcontainer-base` 238, `devcontainer` 555) — the empty-string trap is genuinely closed, and `test_every_stage_that_reads_targetarch_also_declares_it` parses real stage bodies.\n- The matrix `outputs` removal is correct: no workflow still reads `needs.{base-prep,p2996-prep,dev-prep,build}.outputs.*`, and the renamed per-arch artifacts (`build-hash-report-<arch>`, `bootstrap-gap-report-<arch>`) have no downstream consumers.\n- `tag_suffix == arch` for every `PublishTarget`, so `manifest`'s `:dev-<suffix>` and `promote`'s `:dev-<arch>` name the same tags.\n- Both image lockfiles carry `linux-arm64` for **every** tool (45 system / 11 runtime, zero gaps in either direction) — no partial regen.\n- The graphviz apt fallback's version assertion reads `dpkg-query` rather than restating a literal, and its `sed` epoch strip is correct for un-epoched versions.\n- `resolve_bake_platform`'s env→HCL-default order matches bake's, and every hash-computing job exports
